# The purportedly omitted call: what two candidate copies actually contain

October 31, 2026. **The Alderson-attributed public candidate contains a near-matching difficult-time/military-coordinator call and the following conditional Langley-backup discussion.** That is a substantive qualification to a broad omission reading of the staff notes. It does not settle which versions or exact call the notes meant, whether a whole clear call was retained, or whether timing errors were corrected.

The [official interview/review notes, page 17](https://www.archives.gov/files/declassification/iscap/pdf/2011-048-doc8.pdf#page=17), report that a clear ZNY positioning call and some following conversations occur in an original transcript but not Alderson, and require a later MCC Channel 2 timing redo. That warning remains preserved, unchanged and unresolved. The exact versions, source/channel relationship and completed verification remain TODOs.

## Positive passages, not an absence inferred from a search

| Selected passage | Earlier [compiled reproduction](https://www.scribd.com/document/14142009/NYC-Box-2-NEADS-Transcript-Rome-NY-Fdr-Transcript-Pgs-1-82-1237-1417) | [Alderson-attributed candidate](https://www.scribd.com/document/14142047/NYC-Box-3-Neads-conr-norad-Fdr-Transcript-Neads-Channel-2-Mcc-Upside-006) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Difficulty contacting the military coordinator; NORAD wants fighters held over New York | Printed59/NCT0000107, lower block; rows `:53`, `:54`, `:57` after the earlier `13:12:02` label | Viewer22, internal 0052, lines 8–25 |
| Nearby short McCarthy/Nasypany exchange | Printed60/NCT0000108, `13:13:29` through `:41` | Viewer22, internal 0053, lines 5–15 |
| Conditional Langley/tanker/backup discussion | Printed60/NCT0000108, `13:15:04` paragraph | Viewer23, internal 0055, lines 1–7 |

The content sequence gives a better comparison than a single word match. It does **not** authenticate a unique audio event, prove the note's named caller, or establish identical complete calls. These copies may share underlying recordings and are not independent witnesses. No clock offset is calculated; abbreviated old time fields remain abbreviated rather than expanded into invented fully printed timestamps. Exact locators and limits are in `COMPARISON-PINS.json`, OC01/03/05.

The strongest objection to concluding that the staff warning was wrong is that **we have not identified the exact versions or exact call it referred to**. A later/different transcript, another related call, a channel/coverage difference or partial versus complete audibility could matter. These are untested alternatives, not resolved explanations. Conversely, the currently visible content prevents treating the warning as proof that every Alderson-attributed copy lacks this entire sequence.

## A new comparison confound and a local wording difference

**Channel scope:** immediately above the earlier copy's target call, printed 59 has a **Channel 12/MCC-OP** heading. Its DRM number is not clearly supplied in that local heading; we do not fill it in. The previously inspected printed 50 heading was **DRM2/DAT2 Channel 2**, while the other candidate's cover identifies **DRM1/DAT2 Channel 2 MCC Upside**. All three observations remain side by side. Do not automatically assign the printed 50 heading to every later block, or treat this as a demonstrated Channel 2-to-Channel 2 comparison. The additional context does not resolve the DRM-label discrepancy or prove why the note reports an omission. OC02 records the legible local fields, the separate reader rendering and the punctuation/DRM-digit uncertainty beside the prior labels.

**Tanker wording:** old printed 60, the `:37` sentence, displays **A KC10**; new viewer 23/internal 0054, line 16 displays **Two KC-10s**. This is a local text difference in near-matching passages. It is not evidence that one or two tankers were actually ordered or supplied. Neither reading is selected. Source recordings, speaker/context mapping and transcription quality-control records would be needed to test it. OC04 preserves both.

## What was actually read and preserved

Two ordinary reader finds supplied positive locators in both public copies. Actual visual reading then covered selected viewports on old printed 59/60 and new viewer 22/23. The first old59 screenshot was clipped and did not show the target call; it is retained as such. One prospectively documented extra same-page view after ordinary browser zoom exposed the lower call and footer. Old60 was zoomed out with small text and only selected passages adopted, with reader locator support. New22/23 show the relevant internal sections, but parts of those pages remain outside the view. No complete 82-page or 93-page review, whole-version diff or exhaustive following-conversation test occurred. `READING-SCOPE.json` records the actual deviation from the initial viewport cap rather than hiding it.

Five unedited JPEGs remain in the verified native media store. `SOURCES.json` gives each byte count/SHA-256, including the clipped view. Key target views are old59lower SHA-256 `2be27ccbce1866ead7430d0c8343a19f4e436a155c5bc62b908945974787db07` and new22 SHA-256 `9e6680750eec50897d0d45187b1f453840ef8650a6c70c50a75551e86bdc0be2`. These identify **our browser representations**, not native transcript PDFs or historical masters. No native source PDF was acquired or hashed. The official note packet's separately retained hash and previous source readings are distinguished from this unit's preview work.

The previews displayed no reading gate for these views. No signup, download, trial, ad installation, hidden endpoint, source repair or gate workaround was used. Platform AI titles/upload fields and handwritten annotations are not adopted as authentication. Raw screenshots/marked pages/full speech and incidental personal/contact/access/ad details are excluded from the public notes.

## What remains to obtain

Obtain the actual original/Alderson version pair, source/copy/channel manifests and staff call/page referents; then test the whole allegedly missing call and the specific following conversations. Obtain the post-about9:10 redo worksheets, validated output and delivery history. A completed and propagated correction could weaken an integrity concern; an established known uncorrected error in the actual relied-on version could strengthen a narrower one. These candidates establish neither outcome.

`OPEN-TODOS.json` preserves the prior MC/MFR/AV/OM dependencies and original 18 requirements. AV-T02 advances only to **candidate passages inspected**. FAA corrected-QFR/receipt and signed action/no-action/recommendation outcomes, telephone native field/call joins, recorder configuration/custody/clock inputs, materials specimen/native-calibration joins and steel preservation/inventory/attribution/delivery gaps remain open/partial. No calibrated technical-owner test was duplicated. No original criterion is complete from this comparison.

The [preceding report](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/e7e67b0b3ced08a95400a401448dee6dc4ae3a4e/investigation/broader-evidence/neads-alderson-preview-2026-10-31/README.md), conflicting records and all earlier source bytes remain unchanged. `CHECKLIST.md` accounts for the requirements; `SOURCE-CHECKS.json` separates representation checks from historical validation; `TEXT-CHANGES.diff` supplies actual additive text changes. `EDITORIAL-REVISION.diff` preserves the pre-publication qualification of hard-to-read heading punctuation against the retained draft; this changes our precision, not the source. No outreach, fee, request, filing, private-case use, deletion, private push, merge or public-history rewrite occurred. Publication is a separate milestone, not substantive goal completion.

# Port Authority support letter, report use and the missing confirmation

November 1, 2026. Existing B188/H005, AUD011/AUD016, SPT01–SPT08 and SR01. This adds an underlying published letter reproduction to the steel preservation/access inquiry; it does not supply the executed agreement described in earlier testimony.

## The newly read record

The Port Authority's letter to NIST director Arden Bement is dated **January 24, 2002**. Its reproduced office stamp reads **2002 JAN 28 P 2:13**. These are separate fields; neither authenticates historical dispatch, receipt or a calibrated clock. The full image identifies Francis J. Lombard as chief engineer. [Hearing, printed 176](https://books.google.com/books?id=R1GrCiT1US8C&pg=PA176); source PA176 in [SOURCES.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-11a20c4d23ab31a25053).

The letter supports the proposed investigation, describes staff attending the January 10 briefing, and states an understanding restricting report use in damages suits and investigators' expert testimony. It asks NIST to confirm that stated understanding. This is a specific confirmation-request lead, not a recovered NIST acceptance or completed bilateral agreement.

The underlying image and public text both use **submitted** in the report-use clause. The historical statute below uses **admitted**. Both records remain unchanged; no legal effect or cause of that wording difference is decided here.

## Necessary statutory context

The **2000 edition of 15 U.S.C. §281a** already limited use of resulting investigation reports in damages suits. That matters when assessing whether the report-use language was exceptional. The provision's inspected paragraph contains no separate investigator expert-testimony prohibition; this limited textual observation does not establish that no other rule, agreement or advice addressed testimony. [Official historical text, physical 1 / printed 546](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/USCODE-2000-title15/pdf/USCODE-2000-title15-chap7-sec281a.pdf); source USC2000, extraction lines 111–124.

The letter, statute and earlier witness descriptions must be kept separate. No conclusion about enforceability, unlawful restrictions, deliberate concealment or what NIST actually accepted follows from this comparison. The strongest qualification is that the language may reflect existing law or additional guidance not yet acquired.

## Other cooperation records and contrary evidence

The public reader's text groups reproduce FEMA support, NYC Emergency Management's February 22, 2002 letter, and FDNY and city Design and Construction statements about consultation and proposed cooperation. Their accounts add concrete records to the earlier hearing summary. They do not establish that every requested drawing, artifact or access permission was delivered. Full facsimile review of those other letters remains pending; omitted image headers and transcription defects are explicitly retained. [Reader group 161](https://books.google.com/books?id=R1GrCiT1US8C&pg=PA161&output=text), [group 171](https://books.google.com/books?id=R1GrCiT1US8C&pg=PA171&output=text); B161/B171.

These prospective statements qualify any blanket account of permanent total refusal. They do not erase earlier access difficulties, documented processing losses, or the missing item-specific custody and attribution records. They are participants' accounts of the coordinated effort, not independent proof of every operational step.

## What was actually acquired and read

A complete displayed image of printed 176 was downloaded and preserved, with all substantive paragraphs visually checked. Four public plain-text reader groups starting at 161, 166, 171 and 176 were examined; embedded image-only material was not thereby read. One official historical-code PDF was acquired and its relevant paragraph checked against a full-page rendering. This is not a complete facsimile reading of the book or authentication of native correspondence.

The earlier official hearing HTML contains image filenames rather than the letter bodies. That observation remains correct for that representation; it is not proof that the letters disappeared. The complete-book API/download attempts remain unsuccessful and preserved separately. The new image/text readings do not convert those failures into historical evidence. HTML navigation markers must not silently become printed-PDF page references.

[Open records](CITATIONS.md#nov04-1e9b9c2e9f3698a2a5e2) identifies the reply, routing, authority and earlier-agreement joins still needed. A separate [audio method note](CITATIONS.md#nov04-2f10714dd05f053db075) records the recovered local control outputs and why they cannot adjudicate the aircraft descriptions. These are distinct source chains.

[Checklist](CITATIONS.md#nov04-af3d99af34c6aab17363), [verification script](CITATIONS.md#nov04-709ead702b36ac19cf16), [verification scope/results](CITATIONS.md#nov04-c27dce7f8dd71471c55a) and [additive text diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-d9f41d66c1bc224ac034) accompany this supplement. Prior reports and conflicting records remain unchanged. All five work-plan tracks and eighteen substantive requirements remain open or partial; publication does not complete them.

# Steel provenance: exhibition folders and a photographed label

**November 3, 2026.** A newly acquired institutional finding aid supplies two exact archive-folder targets. The folders themselves remain unacquired. A separately preserved public museum photograph did not yield a reliably readable accession number or label transcript, even at its offered original size. Its photographer-written caption adds another unverified attribution, which remains separate from museum records.

This advances the operational steel-record search within **STL01–STL03, AUD011/AUD016, B188/H005 and SPT01–SPT08**. It does not complete the original preservation, attribution, transfer or testing criteria. Beverly Hills certification and approved attachments remain TODOs; their failed access routes were not retried.

## Two precise archive targets

The [New-York Historical Society Museum Department finding aid](https://findingaids.library.nyu.edu/nyhs/nyhs_archives_rg20/all/) identifies collection **NYHS-RG 20**, **Series II. Exhibitions / Subseries II.A. General Files**. NYU hosts the finding aid; New York Historical holds the described archival collection. Neither is the New York State Museum in Albany.

| Catalog entry | Catalog date | Container | Exact saved-HTML locator |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Recovery: The World Trade Center Recovery Operation at Fresh Kills | 2003 | **Offsite-Box 220, Folder 8** | `aspace_ref1945_9j7`, opening tag line 6272; title line 6274 |
| World Trade Center Monument (Research File) | circa 2002 | **Offsite-Box 218, Folder 24** | `aspace_ref1915_gke`, opening tag line 5632; title line 5634 |

The parent description says exhibition files vary substantially. Possible contents include object lists, loans, labels and travel documentation, but those contents are **not confirmed for either folder**. Catalog years generally denote exhibition openings; folder documents can have other dates. Catalog titles may differ from physical folder headings. Most files require advance retrieval from offsite storage. These statements describe the catalog, not a completed access request or inspection. The collection-level restrictions on some other records do not establish that these particular folders are restricted—or that access is guaranteed.

These are documentary leads, not a steel-specimen identification. The exhibition-title association makes Folder 8 worth checking for loan/object documentation; it does not establish that it includes the Albany permanent columns or the previously referenced visual inventory. The generic monument title does not establish which monument, object, institution or metal it concerns. Neither entry links a digital folder body in the acquired finding aid.

## Photograph: caption and label are different evidence

The public [WTC steel-column photograph](https://www.flickr.com/photos/wallyg/55558340145/), attributed on its page to **Wally Gobetz / wallyg**, was actually viewed at **683×1024** and at the offered **4000×6000** size. These are two representations of one photograph, not two witnesses or independent views. The site labels the photograph as taken August 9, 2026; that field is not an independently authenticated capture time.

The caption attributes the displayed double-column fragment to the **North Tower**, describes a **36-foot, three-column assembly originally weighing 20 tons**, and places it on the **west facade, ninth column from the southwest corner, floors 104–107**. These are the photographer's assertions. The caption's source and method of attribution were not supplied or authenticated in this review. Its assembly dimensions are not an analyst's measurement of the surviving fragment.

Two faithful, native-pixel detail crops of the central plinth label and lower-right card were read. Their text is insufficiently legible for a reliable transcript, accession number or member mark. **No label wording was reconstructed from the caption, enhanced by AI, or treated as read.** No material composition, fracture, damage-mechanism or temperature test was performed. The photograph and crops remain local; the public derivative contains no image or cropped-image redistribution. The [offered-size page](https://www.flickr.com/photos/wallyg/55558340145/sizes/o/) identifies the license as [CC BY-NC-ND 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/).

## Keep the descriptions unjoined

| Source role | Preserved attribution | Context and unresolved join |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Museum-authored [phase-two release](https://nysm.nysed.gov/about/press/phase-2-world-trade-center-exhibition-opens-feb-1-nys-museum), captured previously | **North / floors 71-74 / 20-foot / 10-ton** | Recovery; live page carries a January 1, 2003 date field, not authenticated native posting history. |
| Museum-authored [exhibition page](https://nysm.nysed.gov/exhibitions/ongoing/world-trade-center-rescue-recovery-response), captured previously | **South / floors 7–9** | Rescue; no accession join established in the read paragraph. |
| Photographer caption, preserved in this unit | **North / floors 104–107 / 36-foot / 20 tons** | Description of original assembly; no independently read label or object-ID join. |

These values stay side by side without choosing a preferred account. They do **not** establish a contradiction about one physical specimen. Different objects/displays, a loan or replacement, a changed attribution, a descriptive error, and a caption error remain untested possibilities. The strongest objection to a misidentification claim is that neither an identical object nor equivalent measurement scope has been established. Do not count a caption repeating a label as independent corroboration; whether this caption did so remains unknown.

An accession-to-label-to-photograph crosswalk, original identification/marking records and installation/loan history could distinguish the possibilities. No link to **I-0137, W14A/W14B, C12a/b, FEMA Sample 1, a NIST coupon or WTC 7** was established. None of these source observations establishes fabrication, suppression, improper disposal, motive or a collapse mechanism.

## Source pins and reading scope

| Preserved representation | Acquisition or preservation UTC | Bytes | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Complete institutional finding-aid HTML | 2026-11-03T17:47:49.752747+00:00 | 1,364,396 | `44897609eddc23f08ffbf3b94c3b3dea163b02e02d897ed19454a4d3dc0972bc` |
| Displayed photograph | 2026-11-03T17:57:09.971553+00:00, local preservation | 98,495 | `aca21bc0c510634157ec146749c17b0593bdf2242c194127aba31e16f03cdd72` |
| Offered original-size photograph | 2026-11-03T17:57:09.972019+00:00, request start | 4,716,712 | `2b240561df1ba32d024f8d0669e24c5f8d418d247f1a2534aac91e483256562b` |
| Photograph-page browser DOM snapshot | 2026-11-03T18:02:28.217536+00:00, saved-file timestamp | 17,389 | `fe652637303abbd2ecda8d2801ce6320988a7737ae190b402720d051c0169253` |
| Offered-size-page browser DOM snapshot | 2026-11-03T18:02:06.799531+00:00, saved-file timestamp | 4,867 | `22c61246a836146aaf72093b87c8ab9a65e99ae6f36b03c58772655064b1f041` |

Finding-aid entries, collection/series access context and the relevant parent notes were read; the entire collection's described documents were not. Saved HTML lines **794, 804 and 813** locate the parent scope, arrangement and access-note blocks. The caption is at saved browser-DOM line **204**; the taken-date field at **212**; offered size at its separate snapshot line **47**. These are representation-specific locators, not original physical-folder or label pagination. The downloaded HTML and image entity hashes are not original historical records, compressed wire captures or object authentication. Browser-DOM hashes identify that captured representation only.

Four focused queries were used in this bounded unit. The last exact museum/floor-number query yielded no pertinent primary object record among the displayed results; unrelated returns were not adopted. No global absence or archive-exhaustion finding follows. No denied museum/PDF, Beverly Hills or Smithsonian route was repeated. No agency/archive/photographer contact, fee, request, login or physical acquisition occurred.

## Outstanding inputs and preserved versions

**NYSM-05:** Obtain Folder 8's actual contents, including any object list, lender/loan receipt, captions, media inventory and exhibition installation records. Check actual coverage, dates and object IDs; do not presume those contents exist.

**NYSM-06:** Inspect Folder 24 sufficiently to identify its actual subject and supporting records before connecting it to any museum steel. A catalog title alone is insufficient.

**NYSM-01/02/03:** The museum accession/attribution/marking records, native visual inventory and object/loan/label history remain missing. A clear label photograph or original label-design text would help, but cannot alone replace the original provenance chain. **NYSM-04** (actual guide PDF), Beverly Hills certification/attachments and transfer/testing evidence remain TODOs. The original eighteen criteria across all five tracks stay open or partial.

The native sources remain separate on the verified Intel store. The [preceding published review](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/1a1253c81d826ecb148c4b1c53c5fb37cb00c7cb/investigation/broader-evidence/steel-nysm-provenance-lead-2026-11-03/README.md) and its originals are unchanged. ### Archival photograph: steel prepared for transport to the State Museum

<!-- BEGIN REPORT BODY 11d35c231f66ce7c18cd44b00125cac10b7d21470bccba30a4738af6c25a518f -->
# Archival photograph: steel prepared for transport to the State Museum

**November 3, 2026.** A specific New York State Archives photograph and its catalog record provide a new input to the steel-preservation search. The archive dates it **June 2002**, attributes the image to **Mark Schaming**, and captions the load as **North Tower steel ready to move to the New York State Museum**. The preserved, actually viewed image shows a large folded metal assembly resting on a flatbed trailer. Shipment, receipt, original floor and accession identity remain unverified.

This is partial operational-record progress within **STL01–STL03, AUD011/AUD016 and B188/H005**. It does not complete the steel provenance chain or any of the original eighteen acquisition/testing criteria. The FAA correction/disposition originals, Beverly Hills certification and approved attachments, and the other tracks' outstanding native inputs remain TODOs.

## The acquired record and its source references

| Field | Value read from the archival record | Exact saved browser-DOM locator |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Item page](https://digitalcollections.archives.nysed.gov/Detail/objects/45414) title | WTC steel (North Tower) on flatbed ready to move to NYSM | `north-flatbed-page-dom.txt`, line 39; caption line 54 |
| Archival image identifier | **NYSA_22533-13_14_2** | Line 56 |
| Date Original | **June 2002** | Lines 57–58 |
| Photographer | **Schaming, Mark** | Line 60 |
| Source location | **Series 22533-13, Album p.79, top; negative sheet 14, #2** | Line 62 |
| [Collection record](https://digitalcollections.archives.nysed.gov/Detail/collections/6049) | World Trade Center collection and exhibit files; **series 22533** | `series22533-page-dom.txt`, lines 39–49 |

The collection description attributes its Fresh Kills photographs to a New York State Museum curator and describes debris sorting, structural elements and possible collection additions. Its page displays **170 related objects** at line 51. That is a catalog-interface count, not a count of steel specimens, independent witnesses, retained physical pieces or photographs inspected in this review. The collection's database ID **6049**, item database ID **45414**, archival series **22533** and full image identifier occupy different identifier systems.

The album and negative references are unusually useful retrieval coordinates. The underlying album page, negative sheet, original captioning/identification worksheet and camera original were **not acquired**. Their contents and relationship to later accession records remain to be checked. The catalog date and tower attribution are institutional assertions; they were not independently authenticated from a negative, original field notes or identifiable building marks.

## What was actually visible

The acquired display rendition is a **700×457 JPEG**. It shows a folded structural-looking metal assembly on a flatbed. No reliable specimen number, accession label, original member marking or floor identification was read. No scale measurement, alloy identification, temperature estimate or damage-mechanism analysis was performed. Apparent shape alone does not establish tower/floor provenance, when deformation occurred or which later exhibit contains the piece.

The caption establishes that the archive describes the scene as preparation for museum transport. It does not itself establish departure, completed delivery, title transfer, museum accession or present availability. A dispatch/receipt/loan/intake crosswalk would test those later events. A museum destination in a caption also does not establish that the object was a NIST scientific sample or was available for the specific tests previously discussed.

The strongest objection to treating this as a recovered custody chain is the missing item-level join: a photograph with an attributed destination is not a signed transfer or receipt. Conversely, the image is pertinent contrary evidence to a blanket claim that no structural material was retained for potential preservation. It does not answer whether the scientific candidates were adequately preserved or representative.

## Preserve the earlier descriptions without joining them

| Source | Preserved fields | Relationship to the archived load |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Museum phase-two release](https://nysm.nysed.gov/about/press/phase-2-world-trade-center-exhibition-opens-feb-1-nys-museum), previously captured | **North / floors 71-74 / Recovery / 20-foot / 10-ton** | Unknown; no accession or marking crosswalk. |
| [Museum exhibition page](https://nysm.nysed.gov/exhibitions/ongoing/world-trade-center-rescue-recovery-response), previously captured | **South / floors 7–9 / Rescue** | Unknown; different described exhibition section. |
| [Photographer caption](https://www.flickr.com/photos/wallyg/55558340145/), preserved in the preceding review | **North / floors 104–107 / original 36-foot, 20-ton assembly** | Unverified third-party description; its photographed labels remained unreadable. |
| State Archives item **NYSA_22533-13_14_2**, acquired here | **North / June 2002 / ready to move to NYSM** | No floor or accession supplied by the read fields; actual arrival unverified. |

No preferred account was selected. Different objects/displays, a loan or replacement, a changed attribution and descriptive/caption errors remain untested possibilities. Neither common object identity nor equivalent dimension/mass scope has been established. This is not a demonstrated same-specimen contradiction. The records also do not join this load to **I-0137, W14A/W14B, C12a/b, FEMA Sample 1, a NIST coupon or WTC 7**.

## Preservation, access and actual reading scope

The collection and item page were actually read and preserved as browser-DOM snapshots. The exact display-image URL was observed in the public page, retrieved without credentials, preserved and visually inspected. A display-image hash identifies that downloaded rendition, not an original negative, original camera file, physical object or compressed network transmission.

| Preserved representation | Acquisition/preservation UTC | Bytes | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Collection page browser DOM | 2026-11-03T18:19:59.484591+00:00, saved-file timestamp | 12,530 | `f6d7b5f871cf349288be04f7fce91aad1ff07ba3c61cb1626fbd87f61ec6d9db` |
| Item page browser DOM | 2026-11-03T18:21:23.674576+00:00, saved-file timestamp | 3,763 | `aa61e405a39ca8c9cf696e148d9cc82338cf8afb36ae55ab0909fd0e88383494` |
| Displayed image entity | 2026-11-03T18:23:20.834059+00:00, request start | 38,262 | `8d195adc4128f467b1a111a950407450897b860f17f83def19191dc848ea2fb3` |

Only one specific photograph was examined. Partial collection-list metadata was read; the full set of 170 catalog entries and their image contents were not examined or classified. The offered original-download URL returned HTML rather than an image. Its response status/size/hash were recorded, but that failed response body was not saved; no original-image acquisition is claimed. The separately displayed JPEG was accessible and preserved. A collection-export request returned 403, the linked finding aid could not be obtained through the reader, and later index navigation reached a human-verification challenge. Those routes were stopped without bypass or repetition. These access observations do not establish deletion, concealment or altered historical records.

Three indexed queries and one public-interface identifier search were performed. The latter exceeded a literal reading of the unit's initial three-query cap by one; that deviation is recorded, not presented as a passing limit. Subsequent navigation followed observed series-list and item links without more keyword queries or guessed record identifiers. The unrelated object numbered 22533 returned by the item search was not opened or adopted as the archival series. No outreach, fee, request, login, physical acquisition or experiment occurred.

The raw snapshots and photograph remain separate on the verified Intel store. The public derivative contains original notes, links and integrity/locator metadata, with no raw photograph, page reproduction, private case material, correspondence, personal/contact data or credentials. Earlier records remain unchanged. This acquisition does not authenticate the archive's full original-record chain or exhaust its holdings.

## Next discriminating records

**NYSM-07 — archival source chain:** Album p.79, top; negative sheet 14, #2; original caption/field-identification notes and accession metadata for **NYSA_22533-13_14_2**. Compare markings and physical details, preserving uncertain matches and contrary results.

**NYSM-08 — transfer and museum join:** Dispatch/loading/transport documents, actual receipt, intake/accession record, condition photographs, installation/loan history and any later label/attribution versions. Establish separately whether the photographed load reached the museum and whether it corresponds to any earlier described column.

The linked [series finding aid](http://iarchives.nysed.gov/xtf/view?docId=ead/findingaids/22533.xml), remaining image review and any higher-quality source rendition remain TODOs. The State Museum visual inventory, earlier accession/attribution targets, NYHS-RG20 folders **220/8 and 218/24**, Beverly Hills certification/attachments and all original work-plan dependencies are carried forward without closure.

The [preceding published review](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/9a89314d28d911789ff815d9f34fcdb787b690ff/investigation/broader-evidence/steel-exhibition-records-and-label-2026-11-03/README.md) and its native combined version are preserved. An exact additions-only [text diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-f16f1c08ef11397fb94e), [source pins](CITATIONS.md#nov04-f791b1391ceab0c888e9), [observations](CITATIONS.md#nov04-57b0e4f033686679d87c), [provenance leads](CITATIONS.md#nov04-ff7cdcee7ded6d994aaa), [locators](CITATIONS.md#nov04-923b7c8188a72f534ac2), [reading scope](CITATIONS.md#nov04-cd2e7459618e7f2e27ff), [TODOs](CITATIONS.md#nov04-bbdd582e50d8c02d284a), [checklist](CITATIONS.md#nov04-5ac009ba5c1a25aa03fd), [verification](CITATIONS.md#nov04-3e25994d1842383d4ae7) and [bundle hashes](CITATIONS.md#nov04-9aea36ee097ae468144d) accompany this separately named review. Publication verification is not historical, physical or scientific validation.

### New York State Museum: steel descriptions and an inventory lead

**Source unit:** `steel-nysm-provenance-lead-2026-11-03` · [unchanged original body](CITATIONS.md#nov04-27e696fe23f1a7ce34b2).

<!-- BEGIN REPORT BODY 6c41b20bd11375952742bec5e4d0d4a029d239f210039541797f15cd16928e89 -->
# New York State Museum: steel descriptions and an inventory lead

**November 3, 2026.** Three newly read museum-authored pages identify a separate route to retained WTC building material and its documentation. A museum release carrying a **January 1, 2003** date field describes a column attributed to **North Tower floors 71–74**, while the exhibition page retrieved in this study describes one attributed to **South Tower floors 7–9**. Their exhibition contexts differ. The same-object relationship is unknown; these statements do not yet establish misidentification or a changed label.

The most actionable record lead is the 2003-dated release’s reference to a **visual inventory of the museum’s WTC collection**. The native inventory/export, object accession records and marking/transfer files could test the floor/tower attributions and the relationship between these descriptions. None was acquired here.

Existing scope: **STL01–STL03, AUD011/AUD016, B188/H005 and SPT01–SPT08**. This unit does not reopen the deferred Beverly Hills certification/attachments, duplicate telephone or technical investigations, or complete the original steel-custody criteria.

## What the museum sources actually say

| Source and exact captured-text location | Recorded field or statement | Source role and limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [2003-dated phase-two release](https://nysm.nysed.gov/about/press/phase-2-world-trade-center-exhibition-opens-feb-1-nys-museum), captured text line 18 | **north tower; floors 71-74; 20-foot high; 10-ton** steel column. | This paragraph describes **Recovery**, within the announcement of phase two. Museum attribution and reported dimensions/mass were not independently measured. The unit underlying “ton” is unspecified; no conversion was made. |
| Same release, captured text lines 34–35 | **Release Date: Wednesday, January 1, 2003**. | The date is a field on the live page captured in 2026. No original 2003 publication, posting history or native release file was authenticated. |
| [Exhibition page](https://nysm.nysed.gov/exhibitions/ongoing/world-trade-center-rescue-recovery-response), **Rescue**, captured text lines 17–19 | **South Tower; floors 7–9** steel column. | This description appears under **Rescue**. It supplies no accession number in the read paragraph. Current physical presence, original recovery attribution and a same-object connection to the 2003 description were not verified. |
| 2003-dated release, captured text line 20 | A museum computer presentation is described as including a visual inventory of the museum’s WTC collection. | This identifies a particular underlying inventory/data product to seek. The native dataset, images, item keys, coverage and version history remain unacquired. |
| [Collection page](https://nysm.nysed.gov/research-collections/history/political-history/collections/911), captured text line 7 | The institution describes a WTC collection encompassing building materials and other objects. | Collection-level description; it does not identify either column, its accession, source markings or a NIST/FEMA specimen. Its comparative collection-size claim was not tested or adopted. |

The release also mentions an FBI recovery video alongside the inventory. That juxtaposition does **not** establish that the FBI authored the museum inventory, held custody of every listed item or provided the source for each attribution. The inventory and video have separate described roles. We did not acquire or play either.

## Preserve the different accounts and their context

Retain **North / 71-74 / Recovery / 2003-dated release** and **South / 7–9 / Rescue / exhibition page retrieved November 3, 2026** side by side. Both floor and tower fields were directly read in the captured page bodies. No preferred field was selected and no earlier source was edited.

The strongest objection to a mislabeling interpretation is the different exhibition context: **Recovery** and **Rescue** may describe separate objects or displays. Multiple columns, a replacement/loan, a changed attribution or a descriptive error are untested possibilities. The number and identities of distinct physical pieces cannot be inferred from these descriptions alone. The sources were not joined by accession number, marking, transfer event or authenticated object photograph.

These are **unjoined descriptive differences**, not an established contradiction about a single specimen. An accession/loan/exhibit-installation crosswalk and contemporaneous marking/identification records would distinguish the possibilities. A guide explicitly identifying both objects, if obtained, would also matter; its contents have not been adopted from a search snippet or a browser wrapper.

Museum descriptions of retained structural material weaken a blanket premise that no potentially relevant steel was preserved anywhere. They do not establish present availability for sampling, an intact custody chain, representative scientific sampling, a particular heating/damage mechanism or adequate preservation of the scientific candidates. No link to **W14A/W14B, C12a/b, FEMA Sample 1, I-0137**, a NIST retained coupon or WTC 7 was found in the read passages. No material composition, fracture or temperature analysis was performed.

## Next records to seek

1. **NYSM-01 — object identity and attribution:** the museum registrar/History collections’ accession and catalog records corresponding separately to the North 71–74 and South 7–9 descriptions. Obtain original markings/photos, identification worksheets, donor/source information and building/floor attribution basis. Treat a curator’s description separately from its underlying documentation.
2. **NYSM-02 — inventory:** the visual-inventory application/data export referenced in the 2003-dated release, including its original item keys, captions, source-image associations, revision history, coverage and later catalog migration. Do not infer that every object was in the same database or transferred by the same agency.
3. **NYSM-03 — physical and exhibition chain:** recovery/intake/donor/transfer/transport/condition records, loan and installation/removal records, and dated label versions. Test whether the descriptions concern separate pieces, changed displays or altered attributions; retain contrary results.
4. **NYSM-04 — linked educational guide:** the actual complete guide at the [linked public URL](https://nysm.nysed.gov/sites/default/files/2024-10/wtc.guide_.pdf), with edition/page mapping before adopting its contents. It remains unacquired/unread as a PDF. A URL path containing “2024-10” does not authenticate its authorship or publication date.

These are public-record retrieval targets, not sent requests. No contact, fee, FOIL/FOIA submission, loan request, account login, specimen acquisition or test occurred. The institution is a new holder route; no assertion about its response to a request is possible.

## Preservation and actual reading scope

All three declared article bodies were read in the normal HTTPS browser without a security-warning override, credentials or altered TLS protection. The web reader had returned gateway errors and the ordinary downloader failed its local certificate-chain check; neither yielded the requested source body. Those outcomes remain preserved separately. They are not evidence of historical suppression, deletion or source falsity.

The acquired representations are **browser-rendered article DOM captures and derived visible text**, with exact URL, capture UTC, byte count and SHA-256 for each. A DOM hash is not a native 2003 file hash, HTTP response-body hash or compressed wire-byte capture. Two representations of one article are not independent corroboration. Captured-text line references below refer to the saved derivative text, not original historical pagination.

| Article | Capture UTC | DOM SHA-256 | Visible-text SHA-256 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Phase-two release | 2026-11-03T17:28:05.739Z | `8f405b477fcc92f9eee414eacccddaba4c466f095505279216c8cf7c06a041b2` | `34548e695dc7c1d988964954863fc789a500b9e035469e7434a8c585f9bb330b` |
| Exhibition | 2026-11-03T17:28:43.665Z | `3c5320eb644870d9e2f46a51650018a558b0666969c0104679405c8025a31736` | `98eb4ff1a9ed39f57a62e28fada7f86c15fcd2e180f946519c744a1adef932e1` |
| Collection | 2026-11-03T17:29:36.608Z | `a4756cedb1ff8465c64fbfa0ff8863d1c4be0edc62bcf08dec692c776a02d700` | `f8e70ee00fde609aa6ad1716edd68378fca305efd8f3e9115ec4c7b1ce4c06cd` |

Only textual descriptions are used. Artifact/gallery images, videos, audio, native accession records and the actual guide were not examined. Four focused indexed queries and three article reads were completed; displayed search results did not provide an accession join. This is not an exhaustive museum-database search or a finding that records do not exist. The guide capture returned a browser wrapper rather than PDF bytes; no PDF/source-content finding is based on it. Local access/tool receipts remain separate from historical evidence.

The native captures stay preserved on the verified Intel store. The public derivative contains original research notes and source/hash locators, excluding raw DOM/text/images, contacts, personal exhibits, private case/correspondence/strategy, credentials and coordination. Earlier steel records and the immediately preceding combined review remain unchanged.

[Source pins](CITATIONS.md#nov04-646e8576ea09f980f7cc), [observations](CITATIONS.md#nov04-50a3cea5c9d42f5740cc), [provenance leads](CITATIONS.md#nov04-78a23cf541949c3be291), [reading scope](CITATIONS.md#nov04-e8868640adc88a6dd810), [open TODOs](CITATIONS.md#nov04-68d820b32e5ad045ef55), [locators](CITATIONS.md#nov04-8fd5362cbf97bb001222), [checklist](CITATIONS.md#nov04-223487688ea01d5c070b), [verification](CITATIONS.md#nov04-7ae9c72d35746756543f), [bundle hashes](CITATIONS.md#nov04-43053961770fc6962a73) and the additive [text diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-05141117e69c2a3e4f8f) distinguish what was read, inferred, missing and preserved. All original eighteen criteria across the five tracks remain open/partial. Publication verification does not complete original-record acquisition or scientific validation.

<a id="supplement-49ad307d9813f4aa"></a>
### SAVE markings in collected-steel footage: identifier and retention-record leads

**Source unit:** `steel-save-marking-followup-2026-11-03` · [unchanged original body](CITATIONS.md#nov04-c4fe2470d013873c7bb8).

<details>
<summary>Full owning report, including limits, failed checks and TODOs</summary>

<!-- BEGIN REPORT BODY 49ad307d9813f4aa294a170f96249ae4f81a3975950141ce0b0d0138e2fb2cba -->
# SAVE markings in collected-steel footage: identifier and retention-record leads

November 3, 2026. A bounded follow-up to SV03 supplies a **provisional visual reading of “M2” beside “SAVE”**, another visible “SAVE” on an end plate, and measuring-tape activity. A second marking remains partly unreadable. These are leads to identification and handling records, not proof of specimen identity, retention, loss or wrongdoing. Missing originals remain TODOs.

## Source and unchanged earlier reading

The source is the already preserved public **[WTC Tape 3.mov](CITATIONS.md#nov04-805d2455556506e07870)**, reached through [NIST’s image/video navigation](https://www.nist.gov/world-trade-center-investigation/photos-videos-and-simulations) and its [linked collection](https://googledrive.nist.gov/folders/1mO5PPh1ZyZafGUtWrzqhiaDwzTqyEPG-). The October 27 acquisition and November 3 full-file hash check identify **468,978,020 bytes**, SHA-256 **`a74ade3e8cbdf0df5976b67c60ee0a211937440e90d4a29bc211cdfc13057dcd`**. The movie was not downloaded again. The public route and file hash do not authenticate an original camera tape, filming date or physical custody. [Source pins](CITATIONS.md#nov04-0c5184ba34eadb9c98ce).

The [October 27 report](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/75347e9c38e09b5c2037ee9490e6c2c5acf0f799/investigation/broader-evidence/steel-candidate-originals-2026-10-27/README.md#sv03--a-directly-visible-preservation-related-marking), native report lines 27–31, recorded “SAVE” in zero-based input frame **3597**, reported presentation time **240.04004 seconds**, but did not confidently join the preceding identifier to an inventory entry. It expressly left an M/N alias unselected. That report, its native frames and its uncertainty remain unchanged. The new reading comes from additional angles in the same source; it is not an independent witness or an adjudication of the old prefix uncertainty.

## What was actually read

All times below are the decoder’s **presentation timestamps**, not a camera clock, filming date or historical elapsed time. All locators refer to the hash above. [Exact frame metadata](CITATIONS.md#nov04-794ffa261bb2a7182c3a) and [observation locators](CITATIONS.md#nov04-954f4a681cf4b7cfd651) permit independent retrieval.

| Observation | Native zero-based frame / presentation seconds | Accepted observation and unresolved portion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SAVE-O01 | **3630 / 242.242242**; supporting view 3660 / 244.244244 | A marking beside SAVE looks more like **M2** in this angle. This remains a provisional transcription. No source-written hyphen, full catalog designation or inventory match is accepted. The earlier M/N uncertainty remains in the record. |
| SAVE-O02 | **4050 / 270.27027** and **4080 / 272.272272** | **SAVE** is readable on a near end plate. The sampling does not establish whether the two SAVE writings concern one object, separate objects, portions, or one repeated instruction. |
| SAVE-O03 | 4050, 4080, **4170 / 278.278278**, **4230 / 282.282282** | A separate visible long member carries an **MS1/MSI-like token and an N-prefixed string**. The suffix remains unaccepted after the short adjacent-frame extension. No N11, N14, N17 or other complete identifier is assigned. |
| SAVE-O04 | **3660 / 244.244244**, **3990 / 266.266266**, **4080 / 272.272272** | A measuring tape is held across or along visible material. No numerical dimension, calibrated endpoint, result, responsible person or date is identified. |

The M2 candidate was written down **before** the attempted inventory consultation. An inventory label must not be used to supply unreadable characters. Adjacency in a view does not establish that markings on different visible surfaces belong to the same physical specimen. [Observations](CITATIONS.md#nov04-49a43d2fdebf8bd1092b) · [unjoined provenance leads](CITATIONS.md#nov04-bfab50476139a5f12f27).

## Meaning and competing interpretations

The useful lead is a possible route from a visible marking to an intake, marking, measurement or retention record. A SAVE marking could be a handling or preservation direction; its author, date, scope and effect are untested. It does not show whether the whole member or a portion was retained, sampled, transferred or later disposed of. An identifier-shaped marking might also be a field notation rather than a final catalog number. Those possibilities remain separate from the observed lettering.

The strongest objection to any claimed retention failure is that **we have neither an authenticated instruction nor the linked disposition record**. Conversely, a visible SAVE marking or measurement activity cannot prove successful long-term retention, representative sampling or scientific adequacy. The two writings and repeated frames are not counted as independent corroboration or as a count of unique specimens. No link to W14A/W14B, C12, FEMA Sample 1, I0137, WTC 7 or Column 79 has been demonstrated. Nothing here measures material composition, temperature, fracture mechanism, thermite or historical cause.

## Reading and verification scope

The initial selection used `select='between(n,3150,4050)*not(mod(n-3150,30))+eq(n,3597)',showinfo`: **32 native PNGs**. A concrete partly clipped marking then justified a prospectively recorded extension using `select='between(n,4050,4230)*not(mod(n-4050,30))',showinfo`: **7 PNGs**, including duplicate control frame 4050. Both FFmpeg 7.1.1 runs completed with exit status 0, with overwrite disabled and audio excluded.

The **39 files represent 38 distinct input indices**, not 39 independent events. Four initial indices had been extracted previously; 34 indices are newly selected relative to that earlier 16-frame set. Only **11 unique full native frames** were individually viewed: 3480, 3570, 3630, 3660, 3900, 3990, 4020, 4050, 4080, 4170 and 4230. Three labeled contact sheets were viewed for navigation; their thumbnails are not a full-detail reading of every selected image. The initial eight-view limit and the later three-view extension were respected.

The two earlier controls, frames 3597 and 3600, match the new decoded pixels; duplicate 4050 also matches. All 39 PNG hashes/dimensions and three overview hashes were checked. These are reproducible derivation checks, not historical authentication. The original file hash was checked before processing; size and modification time were unchanged after both read-only runs. No continuous visual census, new full-video count, audio review, independent or blinded transcription, AI enhancement, camera-clock test, specimen test or custody authentication was performed. [Reading scope](CITATIONS.md#nov04-2c3bcf1e77b83287df22) · [verification](CITATIONS.md#nov04-ec2baef76a4d30984d66).

## Missing inventory and next discriminating records

The previously recorded NCSTAR 1-3B PDF path was absent from the verified operational media store. That is a specific storage-path limitation; the share was available. One ordinary credential-free request to the [official PDF URL](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/Legacy/NCSTAR/ncstar1-3b.pdf) on November 3 returned **403 / text/plain, 17 bytes**. Its response entity was retained and hashed. It is not a PDF or evidence of a changed report, deleted record or deliberate obstruction. The attempt stopped; no alternate-client or alternate-host retry was made. No inventory page or row was read or adopted in this unit. [Acquisition limits](CITATIONS.md#nov04-0c5184ba34eadb9c98ce).

New TODOs are: **SAVE-01**, original tape/camera and slate/marking crosswalk; **SAVE-02**, actual intake/alias/geometry and inventory records for the candidate markings; **SAVE-03**, original SAVE instruction and subsequent cutting, sampling, transfer, receipt and disposition records; **SAVE-04**, original measurement sheets and any higher-quality source needed to test the unreadable suffix. Each requires a real record, not a presumed match. [Acceptance criteria and carried-forward TODOs](CITATIONS.md#nov04-61e07f3797a3ec255d66).

The earlier Beverly Hills certification/attachments, museum accession and transfer chains, WTC 7-attribution questions and all **18 original-input criteria** remain open or partial. The broader acquisition/testing goal remains **blocked on original inputs**; this available reading is a separate partial research result. No new original-input acceptance criterion is closed.

## Preservation and publication boundary

The movie, earlier frames, reports and conflicting or uncertain readings remain preserved. The [immediately preceding public review](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/c282a14153df29812b7f365e1ae441329666b4b4/investigation/broader-evidence/steel-nysa-flatbed-photo-2026-11-03/README.md) remains unchanged; [REVISION.diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-269a8ae163cb0dfacc65) records only the appended current text against its immediately preceding preserved combined version. ### A second collected-steel movie: readable marking and unequal frame counters

**Source unit:** `steel-tape2-3-source-2026-11-03` · [unchanged original body](CITATIONS.md#nov04-187ab65f75c848d561ec).

<summary>Full owning report, including limits, failed checks and TODOs</summary>

<!-- BEGIN REPORT BODY b7b15309f29a0e90e33807dd79fb2ad45e1296377ab1a75fff2b68bd29981d93 -->
# A second collected-steel movie: readable marking and unequal frame counters

November 3, 2026. The previously unexamined public **WTC Tape 2-3.mov** supplies two useful leads: a visible **N10-C3B** marking and a reproducible difference between a reported frame count and the counts emitted by the selected demux/decode pass. The earlier Tape 3 records and uncertainties remain unchanged. These observations identify records and reproduction questions; they do not establish a building origin, later custody, missing camera footage or deliberate editing.

## Source acquired and preserved

[NIST’s archive entry](https://www.nist.gov/world-trade-center-investigation/photos-videos-and-simulations) led to its [Collected Steel repository](https://googledrive.nist.gov/folders/1mO5PPh1ZyZafGUtWrzqhiaDwzTqyEPG-) and [Video folder](CITATIONS.md#nov04-44eed2826dd2b1a294a3). The preserved seven-item listing identifies [WTC Tape 2-3.mov](CITATIONS.md#nov04-9b5fa90dda92ce3bdf10), ID **1UzY8uR-jH_pmgTzRPok5zgrvB7xfZ4iw**. Current metadata retained the same name and size. We selected it as the next-smallest unexamined file, not because its name proved a chronological sequence or independent source.

The complete anonymous download is **815,698,448 bytes**, SHA-256 **`4beeea4b0e66c5fcaf68f5336ea4b76518f89c6d1cfbba6833915e359f8a4bcb`**, MD5 **`be6770700b7f33e3dd5f07400a809f87`**. Saved bytes were read back and matched the download hash. Retrieval began **2026-11-03T21:34:12.967803+00:00** and finished **2026-11-03T21:36:14.477082+00:00**. This identifies the current public archive entity; it does not authenticate an original camera tape, original filming date or specimen custody. [Source pins](CITATIONS.md#nov04-237b32351be318f15e19).

The Drive tool rejected the file at its 256 MiB transfer limit. An ordinary anonymous download through the actual public large-file warning form succeeded. A local check first overlooked the submit button’s label stored in an input value; that failed attempt was retained and the saved form used without a second initial-page request. There was no sign-in or access-denial bypass. These are acquisition/tool events, not historical evidence of withholding.

## T23-C01 — Reported count versus read packets and frames

| Measurement of this same video stream | Observed value | Scope |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FFprobe metadata field `nb_frames` | **11,607** | Reported field; no independent native sample-table interpretation performed. |
| `nb_read_packets` with `-count_packets` | **11,606** | Packets emitted by this selected FFprobe demux path. |
| `nb_read_frames` with `-count_frames` | **11,606** | Frames emitted by this selected decoder pass. |
| Planned uniformly spaced native images | **12** | Selection used the reported count to propose zero-based endpoint 11606. |
| Images actually emitted by that selection | **11** | Last requested index 11606 did not produce an image in that run. |

The separate count command completed with **exit 0 and empty warning output**, using FFprobe **7.1.1**. Its installed command help was checked for the counting options. The image-producing FFmpeg child completed with exit 0, but the Python checker then failed its expected-12 versus actual-11 assertion. **That original sample-acceptance check remains failed.** The actual eleven images were retained; no substitute endpoint, changed expected value or invented twelfth image was used. [Exact values and commands](CITATIONS.md#nov04-dfec936cb5f93957fce0) · [frame manifest](CITATIONS.md#nov04-80b7f403d3555714ef39).

The counting pass, source hash and failed endpoint test make the difference reproducible for this entity and software configuration. They do not identify its cause. Container/table accounting, edit-list treatment, empty or omitted samples, and demux/decoder behavior are untested possibilities. No native tables or alternate edit/decoder policies were tested here, and no preferred counter or explanation is selected. A difference between these layers is not, by itself, proof of corruption, a lost original frame, a cut, fabrication or an elapsed gap in the filmed events. The original camera/tape, export history, native sample/edit tables and an independent reproduction are needed to distinguish those possibilities.

The [earlier Tape 3 study](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/75347e9c38e09b5c2037ee9490e6c2c5acf0f799/investigation/broader-evidence/steel-candidate-originals-2026-10-27/README.md) reported **6,399 stored/reported and decoded entries** for that different file. That earlier result remains unchanged and was not recounted. It neither authenticates Tape 2-3 nor makes the two files independent corroboration.

## T23-O01–O03 — Field markings and collection context

All indices are zero-based decoder-input indices; times are reported presentation locators, not a filming date or historical clock.

| Observation | Actual full-frame locator | Reading and limitation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| T23-O01 | **8440 / 563.229897 seconds**, frame-09 | Upper line **N10-C3B** is readable on visible structural material. The lower line remains **B-1/8-1 uncertain**. A diagonal line and cross-shaped mark are visible. No inventory, construction-origin or custody join accepted. |
| T23-O02 | **2110 / 140.807474 seconds**, frame-03 | An **M4-like prefix** and an incompletely accepted suffix; lower **S-1/5-1-like** line. Full identifier remains unaccepted. The original partial transcription is retained in OBSERVATIONS.json. |
| T23-O03 | Frames 01,04,07,08,10,11; exact indices/PTS in manifest | Structural members/plates on pavement and racks outdoors, with visible open surfaces, deformation and white markings. No active cutting, measuring, transport, preservation instruction or later disposition established from these sampled stills. |

The readings were recorded before the limited held-text check. A subsequent exact search for N10/C3B in the two held NCSTAR 1-3B page-text derivatives, physical pages 87 and 94, returned no match. That two-page searchable-layer result is not a complete inventory search or proof the identifier is absent. The earlier failed PDF route was not retried. No source label or desired match was used to complete an unreadable character. [Observations](CITATIONS.md#nov04-3b138c76d7d38d2c576b).

The new readable string is a specific target for marking photographs, intake/alias records, retained-piece/coupon crosswalks and source filming logs. It is not an authenticated final catalog number or WTC 7 attribution. Nearby surfaces and labels are not assumed to belong to one specimen. Sparse sampled views do not establish or exclude overlap with Tape 3; file counts and repeated views are not independent witness or specimen counts. The strongest objection to a lost-evidence inference is the absent specimen/instruction/transfer/disposition join. A visible stored member also cannot establish adequate preservation or representative scientific sampling.

## Actual reading, method and limits

Metadata describes a **640×480 Cinepak (`cvid`), RGB24** video, reported rate **2997/200** fps, video duration **774.506507 seconds**, and format duration **774.506667 seconds**. These are separate reported quantities, retained without correction. The format creation tag reads **2010-06-11T17:49:08Z**, the video tag **2010-06-11T17:49:09Z**, and Drive metadata creation/modification **2019-11-26T06:38:59.078Z**. These object fields do not authenticate filming or measure audio/video synchronization. Audio metadata is retained separately; audio content was not listened to or transcribed. [Metadata](CITATIONS.md#nov04-c0c015e51d04e3783b16).

Before content reading, twelve indices were frozen by `floor(k*(11607-1)/11)` for k=0…11: **0,1055,2110,3165,4220,5275,6330,7385,8440,9495,10550,11606**. Only the first eleven produced PNGs. All eleven actual raster hashes and native dimensions were verified. **Eight** were individually viewed: frames **01,03,04,07,08,09,10,11**. One labeled, reduced navigation overview was viewed; its final unused slot says no output and is not a source blank image. Its thumbnails are not full-detail readings of frames02/05/06. No additional interval or full-frame view was used. [Reading scope](CITATIONS.md#nov04-b78839da175c70408c74).

The one full count pass was prospectively added after the concrete endpoint mismatch; it was not an unchanged count rerun. FFmpeg and FFprobe share a library family, so their agreement is not independent decoder validation. No continuous scene census, voice/person identification, AI enhancement, interpolated frame, calibrated physical dimension, camera-clock authentication, material test, building attribution, historical-cause or intent inference was performed. Source size and modification time remained unchanged through extraction and counting; hashes prove entity identity, not historical conclusions. [Verification and failed checks](CITATIONS.md#nov04-9cfa7ff80602efd52139).

## TODOs and preservation

**T23-01:** native sample/duration/edit tables, applicable export/software configuration and independent count reproduction. **T23-02:** original camera/tape and export/source lineage. **T23-03:** actual N10-C3B and uncertain-marking photographs/intake/alias/geometry/coupon/transfer/disposition records. **T23-04:** a source-supported scene/cross-file overlap map if needed. Each remains unperformed; no missing original is silently supplied by the current download. [Acceptance and carried-forward records](CITATIONS.md#nov04-8b5088e9d75f4d3f4f98).

All earlier FAA, telephone, recorder, materials and steel original-input requirements remain open or partial. The [preceding SAVE review](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/71b47a3713297a336e56109e9cea805a8547fa3e/investigation/broader-evidence/steel-save-marking-followup-2026-11-03/README.md), its M/N/M2 readings and all underlying source bytes remain unchanged. The new [REVISION.diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-acf1c62529b9a298ec5f) appends this report to the immediately preceding preserved combined version, with no removed historical text. [Request checklist](CITATIONS.md#nov04-3008b15048f8013ad0f0).

Only minimized original notes, links, hashes, metadata, locators and limitations are published. The movie, audio, frame images, contact sheet, raw warning/documents, personal/contact fields, private case material, correspondence, litigation strategy, credentials and coordination records are excluded. There was no outreach, new request, fee, filing, physical test, private push, deletion, source overwrite or history rewrite. Acquisition, measurement, historical authentication and publication remain separate states.
