# Telephone-record lead: reported FBI cassette custody and a later playback

October 31, 2026 Eastern / November 1 UTC. Scope: the existing telephone-originals work, TEL01–TEL04. This is public-source research, not a finding that the original acquisition or empirical-test requirements have been completed.

## A specific playback and custody record

The [official FBI Vault September interviews, part 04 of 08](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-04-of-08/view), **viewer page 18**, contains a report dated **3/27/02** describing a visit on **March 26, 2002**. Its footer bears **REQ. #35-13** and **000000348**. The body reports:

- A listener used headphones and a cassette player to hear a copy of a cassette tape described as in FBI custody.
- The listener was supplied a transcription. The report describes the recording as a **911 call made on September 11, 2001**, by a person who identified himself as a United Flight 93 passenger.
- The listener reportedly identified the recorded voice. The report subsequently describes other people receiving opportunities to listen and/or read the transcription. It does not report a separate voice identification by every listener.
- Its final paragraph states that the cassette containing the 911 call and its transcription remained in FBI custody.

**What this supports:** a concrete existing-record retrieval target: the cassette/copy and transcription used during that visit, their evidence-item and custody records, the original emergency-call source, and the documentation of the reported voice recognition. This is documentary counterevidence to a blanket claim that there was no retained recording or no reported identification at that time. Such a blanket claim was not independently tested here.

**What remains unknown:** no audio, cassette, master, transcript attachment, evidence barcode, dispatch log, duplication log or signed custody receipt was acquired in this pass. The report's statement of custody is not a complete custody chain. A listener's reported recognition is not an independent audio-authentication or forensic voice test. No native audio analysis or voice test was performed here. The record does not establish a recording's completeness, absence of edits, date of creation, exact event clock, or survival today. No join to the separately reviewed Beamer/operator, Ong/SERO, carrier table or court-exhibit records has been established.

Names and identities masked in this record remain masked. No identity or family relationship is reconstructed by comparing it to other releases. The report, the playback it describes, its final custody sentence and a later reprint must not be counted as separate independent witnesses to authenticity.

## A transcript counterpart: preserve the different release forms

**Part 04 viewer pages 1–2** contain an Ong-labelled transcript header and opening. The header prints **DL-336** and transcription date **09/12/2001**. The introduction describes a taped conversation involving Ong and the Southeast Reservation Center; the continuation bears **09/11/2001** and its opening speaker states **12:28 p.m. Central Time**, introduces a corporate-security role and asks about the morning. The visible reply recalls an incoming call at approximately **8:20**, without a time zone assigned in that passage.

The [previously preserved part 03 observations](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/12173507b1f00e9fdd51e42448dcb2498cc9c4e5/investigation/broader-evidence/telephone-official-part03-2026-10-31/README.md) locate a corresponding header/opening on **viewer pages 2–3**. Those parts also expose different redaction coverage. We preserve the records as different release representations with shared visible text; we have not established full-document or byte equivalence, complete segment mapping, source independence, a change to underlying audio, or the reason for the redaction differences. No masked name or contact field is filled from another copy.

| Item-level observation | Part 03 | Part 04 |
|---|---|---|
| Public viewer's total | 12 pages | 18 pages |
| Item's advertised PDF size | 2,119,910 bytes | 601,286 bytes |
| Viewed DL-336 header/opening location | Viewer 2–3 | Viewer 1–2 |
| Binary obtained in these two source units | No; one direct request returned 403 | No binary request made |

These are container/release and reading-scope observations. They do not show that the difference in totals is missing transcript pages, that two parts have identical contents, or that an underlying recording was altered. In particular, part 04 page 18 is the separately dated playback report described above, rather than evidence that the whole eighteen-page part is one Ong transcript. The adjacent **page 17** is a differently dated September report continuation; adjacency alone does not join its subject or narrative to the March playback.

The broad taped-conversation heading, retrospective opening and separate clock roles remain side by side. No controlling description, time-zone conversion or recording identity is selected. The original audio and transcript-segment/custody map remain TODOs; pages **3–16** of part 04 were not read in this pass.

## Preservation, scope and next records

Four distinct public source pages were inspected: **1, 2, 17 and 18**. Five unchanged browser screenshots were retained locally, including both a larger body view and fitted complete-page view of page 18. Page 2 was read only in its visible portion. Small/unclear footer fields beyond the expressly adopted locators remain unverified. Initial loading frames were not interpreted as blank or missing original pages. No OCR, hidden-layer recovery, direct-download retry, alternate asset, credential or gate workaround occurred.

[SOURCE-PINS.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-8b8e8752cb21fcb95fbd) supplies the official URL, source-page/paragraph references, captured-view hashes and preservation timestamps. **These SHA-256 values identify browser images, not an FBI PDF, cassette, audio master or historical truth.** Raw images, personal/contact information, masked identities, signatures, private case material and correspondence are outside this public payload.

The [TODO list](CITATIONS.md#nov04-8c9baf7f3cdf48341676) retains the playback materials, original emergency-call recording and logs, custody/duplication/transcript records, the DL-336 source/segment map, and the outstanding carrier/operator tables. Individual missing inputs remain deferred while available public-source work continues. All eighteen original five-track requirements remain open/partial; this source-level lead closes none of them. No agency or witness contact, new request, fee, physical experiment, source alteration, private push or history rewrite occurred. Earlier source records and published assessments remain unchanged.

# Two Ong-labelled transcripts: fuller printed comparison, unresolved audio provenance

The released FBI packet contains additional printed differences beyond the previously recorded **passenger/purser** wording. In corresponding injury passages one account prints **“I'm number five.”** and the other **“Our number 5,”**. The repeated cockpit-access question is assigned to different printed speakers. A nearby question and a **Somebody/nobody** clause also differ. These observations concern the released **transcriptions**, not independently heard speech. Neither wording or speaker assignment is selected as correct.

## Source, boundaries and actual work

The unchanged [official FBI September part04 PDF](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-04-of-08) has18pages/601286bytes, SHA-256 `854bf3408241640a4b5549fba79b7a1f8a9a2639267488b9db3132bd08ac3caf`. It was acquired November2,2026 and previously read for selected findings. This new unit compares the displayed passages end to end; it is **not a new source acquisition** or a claim that the pages were never viewed before.

- **A: DL-336**, physical3–7, footers000000402–406. Page2 supplies the retrospective interview/playback context. The line preceding playback on3 and the post-**Recording concluded** interview on7 remain separate from airborne-playback content.
- **B: DL267/CE66**, physical11–14/internal2–5, footers000000458–461. Its cover on10 reports CD-ROM-to-analog copying for transcription. The CD, tape and copy/session logs remain unacquired.

All nine comparison page images and both context pages were directly inspected. Source and reused-raster hashes matched prior pins. [SOURCE-PINS.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-564a76a0d9ee1dd7a1d0) gives exact source/page/footer locators; [PASSAGE-MAP.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-fe82aa5e526dee460579) covers the consecutive displayed passage groups and uncertain relations. Analyst IDs are navigation labels, not invented original line numbers. This is a passage-level review, not a certified word-perfect transcription or exhaustive punctuation census. No new listening, voice identification, waveform alignment or clock fit occurred.

## Differences requiring source-media checks

| ID | Printed record A | Printed record B | Qualification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D01 | Physical5/footer404: **I'm number five.** | Physical12/footer459: **Our number 5,** | Corresponding injury/galley/cockpit context; earlier and later number-three identification remains in both records. No actual identity or contradictory self-identification inferred from the printed difference. |
| D02 | Physical6/footer405 places the repeated cockpit-access question under a redacted label and its reply under **ONG**. | Physical13/footer460 includes both question and reply in one **FA ONG** paragraph. | The printed attribution differs. This cannot identify the actual voices or establish an extra participant. |
| D03 | Physical6: **Can they not see the girls get upset?** The earlier fragment on4 instead prints **(UI) Did you seen the girl who got stabbed?** | Physical13: **Would they not maintain a sterile cockpit?** | The later comparison follows the door-closed fragment/apology and precedes the guys-up-there reply. Earlier A4 alignment remains nonunique. Neither question is adopted as heard speech. |
| D04 | Physical6: **Somebody can call the cockpit.** Earlier physical4 prints **Nobody can call the cockpit to see if we can get inside.** | Physical13: **nobody can call the cockpit,** | Preserve all three readings. They do not establish whether a cockpit call occurred or succeeded. |
| D06 | Physical5 uses **he's coming back** and a generic unintelligible-background marker. | Physical12 uses **somebody is coming back** and includes explicit background speech before a separate talking marker. | Pronoun/coverage difference; absence from a printed span is not demonstrated absence from original audio. Names in the background line are omitted from this derivative. |
| D07 | Physical5: **our passenger is stabbed.** | Physical12: **our purser has been stabbed.** | Previously documented in the [generation-chain review](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/17aef02c7d79169234dc04c4a67f38425900ea30/investigation/broader-evidence/telephone-transcript-generation-chain-2026-11-02/README.md), retained rather than counted as new independent evidence. |
| D08 | Early physical3 prints **We can't even get a manager to the cockpit.** Later physical6 prints **We can't even get into the cockpit.** | Physical13 also prints **We can't even get into the cockpit.** | The early fragment cannot uniquely be time-matched; the later agreement is counterevidence against portraying every passage as conflicting. |
| D09 | The later reservation query has a first-name speaker label; earlier labels are redacted. | Corresponding physical13 labels use **OP Agent** and additional explicit confirmation turns. | A label/coverage difference, not a recovered identity or verified call-leg join. |
| D10 | Physical7: **No, we're just calling medical and we can't get**; then **Recording concluded.** | Physical14: **No. Somebody is calling Medical and we can't get**; then **End of tape**. | Both printed lines stop at **get**. No missing duration or native recording endpoint is measured here. |

The exact selected readings, page/paragraph anchors and limits are preserved in [DIFFERENCES.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-713bfd463942773118b8). D05 separately records the sequence/coverage issue below. Ten register entries are analyst observations, not ten calls, edits, witnesses or independent confirmations.

## A sequence issue, without a claimed explanation

A begins with breathing/description/cockpit/Flight12–11 material on3, followed by reservation and seat questions on4; similar topics occur again in its longer run on5–6. B begins with a number-three/cockpit/mace/hijack opening on11, then seat/name material and the longer overlapping sequence on12–14. The early A fragments therefore cannot all be assigned a unique one-to-one position in B from words alone.

The map preserves the early fragments and the later counterparts instead of deduplicating them. It does **not** establish replay, looping, a deliberate edit, two different calls, a master-file omission or what produced the displayed structure. Similar statements may be repeated in an actual conversation; playback/session handling, source coverage and transcription are additional untested possibilities. The first **Flight12** attributed to FA ONG in B11 is distinct in the printed sequence from the later AA/OP Flight12 exchange in B12. Matching that number alone would create an unsupported alignment.

## Agreement and the strongest competing reading

The seat/**3R**, name/number-three/**Flight11**, Boston–LosAngeles, later flight-number correction and medical-contact ending supply substantial ordered parallels. Both later access passages use **into the cockpit**, and both terminal medical-contact lines end at **get**. These agreements constrain a claim that the printed differences demonstrate wholly different calls. They still do not prove identical native recordings, authenticated voices or calibrated clocks.

An ordinary source-generation/transcription account could produce differences of wording, speaker labels and displayed coverage. That account is plausible, **not demonstrated**. Conversely, the paper differences alone do not establish fabricated or deliberately altered audio, attack participation, concealment or motive. Related transcriptions and their handling descriptions cannot be counted as independent authentication of the underlying call.

## Next discriminating records and remaining scope

The useful targets are the actual A interview/playback recording with its full session/seek history, the B-source CD and analog rerecording with duplication/transcription logs, and the native call/master plus segment/channel mappings. Compare authenticated samples at the injury, cockpit-question and Somebody/nobody passages; retain unaffected seat/route and terminal passages as controls. Then determine whether the two written accounts represent identical source samples, different coverage or transcription choices. These tests remain **unperformed**.

The prior engineering inquiry, effective recorder configuration, faxed statements and receipt, original notes, carrier/operator/transfer/recipient links and clock definitions remain TODOs. The [prior report](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/17aef02c7d79169234dc04c4a67f38425900ea30/investigation/broader-evidence/telephone-transcript-generation-chain-2026-11-02/README.md) and all original conflicting values remain unchanged. [OPEN-TODOS.md](CITATIONS.md#nov04-9e8479a5a254fc143d31) carries all five tracks/eighteen original requirements forward. This comparison completes only the declared printed-passage review, not any native-input acceptance criterion.

[CHECKLIST.md](CITATIONS.md#nov04-3d2ebec104868603487e), [REVISION.diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-af7b7fae138f2d0869eb) and [VERIFICATION.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-5a7de96e158079fea7f4) distinguish actual reading, preservation checks and remaining tests. No raw scan, full transcript, audio, signature/contact field, redacted identity or private case material is included in this derivative. Publication does not establish historical accuracy or main-branch adoption.

# Telephone originals: a recipient interview and an answering-machine lead

October 31, 2026 Eastern / November 1 UTC. The [official FBI September interviews, part 05 of 08](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-05-of-08/view) supplies two source-specific retrieval leads. These are released interview/form representations, not acquired native carrier tables or audio. They do not complete any of the eighteen original five-track requirements.

## 1. Recipient report CE40: separate recollection, belief and caller statements

**Viewer pages 25–26**, internal pages 1–2, are an FD-302-style report with **Lead Control Number CE40**, transcription date **09/12/2001**, and **REQ. #35-13** footers **000000151–000000152**. The caller is identified in the visible text as Sandra Bradshaw. The recipient's masked identity remains masked.

| Source location | What the report actually says | What has not been established here |
|---|---|---|
| Page 25, incoming-call paragraph | A call was received between **9:30 a.m. and 10:00 a.m.** on September 11. | A measured switch timestamp or a time zone. No zone is supplied in that passage. |
| Page 26, second paragraph | The recipient **believes** the call used a **GTE telephone mounted on the back of an airplane seat**. | Verified device, carrier/service routing or a match to a native billing/switch row. |
| Same paragraph | The call was **estimated** to last **five to ten minutes**. | An independently measured duration or actual start/end pair. The thirty-minute receipt window and duration estimate describe different quantities. |
| Same paragraph | The connection was described as poor; the wording includes inability to hear background conversations or noise, followed by mostly hearing **air noise**. | A recording-based acoustic result or an inference that other passengers were silent or absent. Preserve both descriptions without choosing or rewriting one. |
| Pages 25–26, relayed narrative | Caller statements include hijackers, passenger movement and an asserted flight location. The page-25 account also describes crew location as an **assumption** and says the first-class curtain obstructed the caller's view. | Direct observation by the recipient, independently measured aircraft position or direct sight of the cockpit. |
| Page 26, notification paragraph | The recipient reportedly called **United Airlines in Newark immediately afterward** and spoke to an **FBI Chicago agent later**. | Original outgoing call records, airline intake/incident log, agent-contact record, independently established sequence or a calibrated time. |

Page 25 also reports **27 passengers at the back** with the caller. This remains a relayed, phase-specific account, not a manifest census or an independently verified count. It must not be combined with other calls' headcounts as though every account describes the same moment and population. No row-level accounting was performed from this narrative.

The most discriminating next records are the original recipient interview notes, any source recording, call-specific carrier/operator records and event definitions, and the reported United/FBI notification records. Those could test the service belief, clock, duration and chronology in either direction. They have not been acquired here. Unclear footer fields outside the adopted locators are left untranscribed rather than silently regularized.

## 2. SF2442: an answering-machine message is described, not produced

**Viewer page 16** opens a form/report dated **09/25/2001**, citing **lead control number SF 2442**. Its **page 17 “Last Contact” field**, footer **REQ. #35-13 / 000000273**, says **Lauren Grandcolas** left a message on a telephone answering machine at approximately **6:00 a.m., Pacific Standard Time**, on **September 11, 2001**.

Preserve the approximation and printed **Pacific Standard Time** label. No PST/PDT substitution, UTC conversion, alignment to a desired flight chronology or comparison to another caller's clock was performed. The field is a reported last-contact description, not a native answering-machine timestamp or proof that its clock was set correctly. This unit supplies no independently verified conflicting event time.

**Concrete original-record target:** the answering-machine recording, original device/export metadata, any contemporaneous transcription and the records of its collection, copying or examination, if such records exist. The form does **not** say the FBI acquired or retained the recording. The inspected continuation on page 18 adds contact/support information, rather than the sought recording or a collection receipt. No raw message or custody attachment was found in these inspected form portions; this is not a claim about every other page, file or repository.

This differs in evidentiary role from the [previously preserved March 2002 playback report](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/abefdac15a5474b2cba2407dc7d0e6bcd58ead59/investigation/broader-evidence/telephone-playback-custody-2026-10-31/README.md), which expressly reports a cassette and transcription remaining in FBI custody then. **No same-recording, same-caller or custody join between these reports has been established.** A description of a message does not inherit the other report's custody assertion.

## Scope, preservation and outstanding tests

The official item advertises **1,097,079 PDF bytes** and its viewer exposes **26 pages**. Six distinct source pages were inspected: **1, 16, 17, 18, 25 and 26**. Page 1's photographic-identification interview is outside the adopted telephone findings. Only the relevant header of the personal/background form on 16 was adopted; personal/contact fields and unrelated narrative were excluded. Larger and fitted views of 25–26 support the selected paragraph/footer readings. Pages **2–15 and 19–24** were not read; neither thumbnails nor loading frames establish their content or absence of attachments.

Seven unmodified viewer images were retained locally. [SOURCE-PINS.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-87e38d068d557f4f434c) records the official URL, selected page/paragraph/footer locators, SHA-256 values and preservation timestamps. These hashes identify **captured browser views**, not an original FBI PDF, audio, answering-machine device or authentic historical event. No PDF was requested or obtained, and no audio was acquired, played, decoded, transcribed or forensically tested. No new OCR, hidden-identity recovery, failed-download retry or access workaround occurred.

[OPEN-TODOS.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-e5e75018d9049d481ff6) carries the original-message and recipient-record dependencies alongside all existing FAA/telephone/recorder/materials/steel requirements. No witness/agency contact, new request, fee, experiment, mailbox access or private case-material use occurred. Raw images, contact details, signatures, masked identities and private correspondence are excluded from the public payload. Earlier records and published assessments remain unchanged; no conspiracy, fabricated-call, suppression or current-retention finding follows from these source limitations.

# Initial call recipient: statement, observation limits and reported restrictions

A complete four-page interview in an already-held public packet provides a concrete lead to a separate statement reportedly written after the call. It also reports an instruction to **“block it”** concerning unspecified system information. The same account expressly limits what the recipient heard and what she merely assumed. These are attributed reports in a reproduced interview, not the native statement, telephone events or proof an instruction was implemented.

## Source and reading scope

The [public mirror](https://www.les-crises.fr/wp-content/uploads/2016/06/Team7-Box13-Flight-Call-Notes-and-302s.pdf) has 73 pages and 2,997,662 bytes; SHA-256: `e3cf374351cb753d1297433f86bb5807c56cb42a6dc69837a9df02dd0d54f4aa`. It was acquired October 27, 2026 and remains unchanged. This November 2 examination is a scoped reading of held material, **not a new acquisition or independently authenticated native agency production**.

The recipient interview occupies physical **38–41**, internal **1–4**, footers **000000447–450**, lead controls **DL267/CE66**, case **265D-NY-280350-CE**. Its transcription/investigation/dictation fields read **09/12/2001**. All four page faces were read through the end for the business, call and source passages adopted below. Biography, family/contact details, obscured identities and unverified marginal annotations are excluded.

The distinct two-page booking report at 42–43 was viewed to establish its complete boundary and check a tentative year reading. Page 44 is a different phone-analysis record, used only for its header/boundary; its financial/identity/call fields are not newly adopted or reproduced. Seven full-page images were displayed, with three detail rechecks on 38, 41 and 42. Those rechecks are not additional records. [SOURCE-PINS.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-bf4fdc51a49223f14adf) records the source URL, original access date, hashes and exact page/footer scopes. No whole-packet reading, hidden-text recovery, OCR, audio listening, ASR or native clock/system test is claimed.

## A separate statement to locate

[Physical 41, first body paragraph](https://www.les-crises.fr/wp-content/uploads/2016/06/Team7-Box13-Flight-Call-Notes-and-302s.pdf#page=41) says a supervisor directed the recipient to write a statement and be available for debriefing. It reports that she wrote the statement in a conference room. That airline statement is a different object from this FBI-form interview. The reproduction does not supply its original bytes, content/version or receipt.

Earlier work identified separately listed operator statements, notes, recordings and prospective 1A transfers. This is a closer creation-context pointer, **not proof that a listed enclosure is this exact statement or that a promised transfer occurred**. An actual content/date/version/custody match is needed; names or shared case/control labels alone do not join the objects.

## Line setup and partial call coverage

[Physical 38–39](https://www.les-crises.fr/wp-content/uploads/2016/06/Team7-Box13-Flight-Call-Notes-and-302s.pdf#page=38) reports an inability to find the emergency button, speed-dial contact with the international resolution desk, opening the line to three participants, a colleague activating his button and an operations participant indicated by a light. It describes an operations handoff. The account explains the button as triggering recording and alerting operations. These are reported actions/functions, not native switch, recorder configuration or authenticated call legs.

Reservation-side actions do not settle the separate later dispatch **patch/relay** accounts. The source's recalled first words also differ in source role from the opening of the [previously inspected printed recording transcription, part 04 physical 11/footer 458](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-04-of-08#page=11). No common first sample, actual trigger time or cause of the coverage difference has been established.

[Physical 40, third body paragraph](https://www.les-crises.fr/wp-content/uploads/2016/06/Team7-Box13-Flight-Call-Notes-and-302s.pdf#page=40) says she **“did not hear the end”**. It describes a headset handoff and estimates more than 20 minutes before another listener took over. Her participation estimate is not total call duration, recorded duration or a calibrated crash/disconnect time. The account gives crash or lost communication as alternatives after the other listener removed the headset; neither is selected here.

The preceding paragraph reserves whether seat references were heard during the call or later operations discussion, and whether an exclamation was heard directly or learned afterward. Those qualifications prevent treating the reported details as direct last words or an independent seat identification. Reported repetition in conversation also does not explain or authenticate particular repeated passages in the earlier printed comparison.

## Caller reports and recipient assumptions remain separate

[Physical 39, second and third body paragraphs](https://www.les-crises.fr/wp-content/uploads/2016/06/Team7-Box13-Flight-Call-Notes-and-302s.pdf#page=39) reports an initial **Flight 12** reference, then **Flight 11, Boston to Los Angeles**. It expressly says the recipient **assumed** the references to **#5 and #1** meant other crew members. It says she **thought** the caller was relaying information provided by others and did not believe the caller could actually see events.

Those are expressly marked interpretations in a witness account, not authenticated voice, crew identity, injury or direct-observation findings. They qualify how much weight this report can bear in assessing the earlier **“I’m number five” / “Our number 5”** printed conflict. Both printed versions remain preserved; this interview does not establish which words were spoken. Shared case controls and related source chains also prevent counting these pages as automatically independent corroboration.

## Preserve reported times without joining clocks

| Source and role | Account | Missing link |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Recipient interview, physical 38/footer 447, last body paragraph | **Approximately 7:59 a.m.** received a call | Native event/time source, timezone and clock calibration. |
| Same interview, physical 41/footer 450, third body paragraph | Computer lookup reportedly said Flight 11 **took off from Boston at 8:04 a.m.** | Actual terminal/status record, field definition and timestamp basis. |
| Previously read [official part 04, physical 2/footer 401](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-04-of-08#page=2), retrospective account | **Approximately 8:20**: an employee receives a call; emergency alert and coordinator listening are described | Which event, leg and clock each time denotes; a common source match. |

The **7:59** and **8:04** strings were checked in visible-page detail renders. They remain as printed, without selecting either as historical time. The **8:20** account is reused context, not a new measurement. Morning-time zones and native joins are unestablished. **No delay, common call-start time or call-before-takeoff finding is calculated from these unjoined descriptions.** Native agent/telephone events and the original terminal flight-status record would help test their relationship.

## Information-control instructions: concrete lead, unknown execution

[Physical 41, second body paragraph](https://www.les-crises.fr/wp-content/uploads/2016/06/Team7-Box13-Flight-Call-Notes-and-302s.pdf#page=41) reports overhearing discussion of information still accessible in a system, followed by an instruction to **“block it.”** The report adds **“apparently to keep it from being seen by others.”** That qualification remains the report's interpretation, not a verified purpose.

The precise information, system, object, scope, authority, recipient and actual execution are unestablished. Nearby manifest/ticket discussion does not identify **it**. This is not an audit log, deletion record or demonstrated change. Authorized incident privacy/security restriction, improper suppression, and a misunderstood or unimplemented instruction remain **untested alternatives**. None is selected to resolve or dismiss the report.

Discriminating inputs are the actual instruction and recipients, target data/system, effective incident/access policy, permissions/change/audit/retention logs and surviving relevant data or snapshots. None is recovered here.

The last paragraph of the same page says she tried not to discuss the call because she had been told not to. The issuer, exact words, time, scope and authority are unspecified. It is not identified as a legal gag order or joined to the block-it instruction. **The reported direction to write a statement and be available for debriefing remains alongside the reported discussion restriction.** That documentation account limits a simple claim that all documentation was stopped, while not establishing whether access/discussion restrictions were proper or improper.

## Our rejected year reading

An initial low-resolution reading of the adjoining booking report suggested **2011**. A preserved detail recheck shows **2001 in both booking-date passages** on physical 42; its form date is **09/13/2001**. Our preliminary note and correction are retained locally. Source bytes did not change. This is **our reading error, not conflicting historical records**; no year-discrepancy lead, booking identity, payment or nationality inference is adopted.

## Status and next inputs

The advance is a source-level map to a reportedly created airline statement, reported line/recorder actions, partial listener coverage, explicit assumptions and memory limits, and unverified access/discussion instructions. [OBSERVATIONS.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-440825a07f666e04dddc) contains nine related analyst observations, not nine witnesses or independent confirmations. Original statement/version/receipt, end-listener notes, native call/session media, switch/agent events, terminal data, effective policies and execution/custody records remain missing.

No covert role, fabricated call, deliberate alteration, executed deletion, conspiracy or motive is established. Prior sources, printed disagreements and the failed synthetic control remain unchanged. [OPEN-TODOS.md](CITATIONS.md#nov04-73e27ab0a45e7cc40fcf) retains all five tracks and 18 original requirements, including the priority FAA corrected answer/receipt, signed administrative decision and separate recommendation disposition. None is accepted by this reproduced interview.

[CHECKLIST.md](CITATIONS.md#nov04-75d536cc50de83f21265), [REVISION.diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-96613df0217a6029e58b) and [VERIFICATION.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-411cb9f91edea6b321cf) distinguish actual reading from byte/navigation checks. This derivative excludes raw scans/images/full interviews, private case material, biographies, personal/contact/payment data, signatures and reconstructed identities. Publication integrity is not historical validation, independent audit, substantive goal completion or main-branch adoption.
<!-- END REPORT BODY 45cc8d533013c3d1e142cfd19edc89546dd68e6d8a58b321574320e2a4f5f9af -->

</details>

### A reported cassette handover and two different telephone relay accounts

**Source unit:** `telephone-recording-custody-relays-2026-11-02` · [unchanged original body](CITATIONS.md#nov04-4b7b7aef9d8208ea7af0).

# A reported cassette handover and two different telephone relay accounts

November 2, 2026, America/New_York; source-reading and verification receipts extend into November 3 UTC. TEL01–TEL04 / AUD016-A1 / TC05-B105 / TC06-B110. These are newly inspected pages in already-held public FBI records, not newly acquired tapes or independently authenticated calls.

## 1. A concrete Boston cassette-custody target

[Official part 01, physical page 11](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-01-of-08/at_download/file#page=11), production footer **000000002**, contains an FD-302 reporting collection of an answering-machine cassette associated with **Linda Gronlund**. The report describes these separate events on September 11, 2001:

| Printed approximate time | Reported event | What is not established |
|---|---|---|
| **6:45 p.m.** | Writer telephonically contacted; told a message was on cassette and would be made available to the FBI. | This is the reported contact time, not the time the original message was recorded. |
| **7:30 p.m.** | Writer interviewed the recipient and received the cassette recording from the answering machine. | No separately recovered evidence item, receipt, native recording identity or original-versus-copy determination. |
| **9:20 p.m.** | Writer transported the tape directly to the FBI office in Boston and handed it to an assistant special agent in charge. | No separately recovered intake/transfer record, examination result, later disposition or present-custody proof. |

All three times remain as printed, with **no adopted time zone**. No elapsed interval, call time, aircraft time or calibrated clock is derived from them. The form prints transcription **09/12/2001**, investigation **09/11/2001**, dictated **09/12/2001**, file **265D-NY-280350**. Those are different field roles. The redacted recipient/author and personnel identifying fields are not reconstructed or reproduced.

This is a more specific retrieval target than a generic missing-recording allegation: the institution, receiving role, date and reported handover are identified. It is still an interview/collection report's account, **not the cassette itself or a complete evidence chain**. The actual receipt/item record, original/copy history, examination/transcription and retention/disposition records could corroborate, qualify or contradict the account.

No explicit item/control cross-reference joins this cassette to the earlier **BA264/IN3221 pickup, HQ1492, HQ366 or WF334** leads. Similar recording descriptions, caller associations or a common investigation are insufficient. Their earlier differing names, dates, AM/PM fields and assignments remain unchanged. [Preserved prior comparisons](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/56e7780d85b9947de12f36dfe614ac1794d3ff78/investigation/broader-evidence/telephone-pickup-hq1492-disposition-2026-11-02/README.md).

## 2. An attempted Beamer patch became a reported party-line relay

[Physical page 43](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-01-of-08/at_download/file#page=43) and [continuation 44, internal page 2](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-01-of-08/at_download/file#page=44) contain a different FD-302, dated **09/11/2001**, file **265D-NY-280350**. The opening identifies a call from a **GTE Airfone office in Oak Brook**, relaying information from another employee reportedly speaking to **Todd Beamer**. It does not begin as a demonstrated direct FBI-to-passenger conversation.

Preserve the following reported sequence without selecting a calibrated timeline:

- **Approximately 8:45 AM:** contact to the special agent and a relayed passenger account.
- The office was trying to patch Beamer's call to the agent; GTE employees could not accomplish that immediately. The report says **that office lacked the capability to record Beamer’s conversation**. It does not establish that no recording existed anywhere in the wider provider/recipient network.
- **9:03 AM:** the intermediary said he believed the call had disconnected.
- **9:11 AM:** the intermediary said the call had **not** disconnected and that the phone might be in a seat pocket.
- **Approximately 9:15 AM:** a patch was described as ready, but the report says a **party-line call ensued instead**, introducing a New York State Police participant. The continuation describes a separate **Jeremy Glick-associated** telephone relay.
- **9:36 AM:** the continuation says both intermediaries reported losing the calls. This is a reported endpoint, not an authenticated switch termination or aircraft event.

No time zone is printed in these adopted labels. The **believed disconnected / not disconnected** reports remain side by side as source statements at different stated moments; no cause or actual network state is chosen. Readiness of a patch is not proof that the intended passenger-to-agent patch was completed. Conversely, this report does not establish that no direct connection occurred at any other point. The masked identities and cross-call speaker/leg assignments remain unfilled.

The next discriminating records are the actual operator and FBI/NYSP call logs, bridge/transfer tickets, recordings if made, circuit/leg identifiers, field definitions and source-clock configuration. Those are needed before joining any printed carrier row to a passenger, operator, party line, silent interval or termination. The three-hijacker/weapon and passenger-count descriptions in the narrative are relayed statements, not independent physical or caller authentication; they are not newly used here to establish events aboard an aircraft.

## 3. A separate recipient report asserts a recording and a later endpoint

[Physical page 12](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-01-of-08/at_download/file#page=12), footer **000000003**, is a **different** FD-302 concerning a redacted recipient of a **Glick-associated** call. Its form prints transcription, investigation and dictated dates **09/12/2001**, while its narrative describes **09/11/2001**, sometime after **9:30 a.m.** Those roles and dates remain distinct.

The narrative reports hearing sounds, then silence, and an operator relaying information to police. It presents the recipient as sure there was a tape recording of the conversation and writes the provider-name variants **“Varizon, or Verizon.”** Neither the affiliation nor the recording's existence/content was independently verified here; the spelling is not silently standardized or used to reconstruct a masked person.

It reports the line remaining open until **approximately 10:45 a.m.** No printed time zone is adopted. Preserve that account beside the **9:36 AM** loss report on page 44 and the previously reviewed operator's speech/silence/provider-relay estimates. **Do not treat those as synchronized endpoints of an established common leg.** Clock basis, source identity, routing and which connection each witness meant are unverified. No preferred time, conversion, historical gap or causal explanation is selected.

The recipient's recording assertion and the GTE office's lack-of-recording-capability statement concern differently attributed accounts and potentially different offices/legs. They do not prove a single contradictory recording state. Nor does a possible different-leg explanation supply the missing record. Provider, recorder, police/office and custody records could strengthen or weaken either account. No new audio was acquired or listened to.

## 4. Boundaries, index check and what was actually tested

Five distinct page faces were visually inspected at **140 dpi**: **10, 11, 12, 43 and 44**. Page 10 is a different **CVR transcript page 10 of 10**, not a cover or continuation of the cassette-collection report. Its technical content was excluded from this telephone study; no recorder timing/translation work was duplicated. Page 12 is a separate report rather than a continuation of page 11. No complete 49-page visual reading or original-record authentication is claimed.

The held ordinary-text index had useful topic hits at pages 11 and 43. An exact check of the already-held public court prosecution index found **no literal Gronlund or Glick row match**. Its two generic cassette-title matches do not identify either requested recording, so neither was opened or adopted as related. This is a bounded check of a particular index representation, not an exhaustive no-record finding. No external query or new source download was needed for this unit. [Search/locator scope](CITATIONS.md#nov04-b1b865ba1239e54f462f).

The original PDF remains **4,468,234 bytes, 49 pages**, SHA-256 **4760de107bef3c75f4d7629f90f7a70d2c861cb3b53dcbe37a3bf0fb768f93cb**. [SOURCES.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-16d4d7ab6d9b24589f4c) pins its original access time, relevant paragraph/field locators and the reused court-index entity. Source bytes and selected render hashes were verified. The automated checks reproduce topic/index matching and bundle integrity; **manual image readings are not independently verified by that code**. No hidden text, mask recovery, new OCR, voice/caller authentication, native carrier decoding, synchronized clock fit or physical test was performed.

The source reports, potential tape, agency transfer account, court index, caller relays and later recollections are different evidence stages. They must not be counted as independent corroboration of wrongdoing. High confidence attaches to the selected visible readings; the historical message, recording/copy identity, actual leg/routing, source clocks and complete custody remain unverified. Nothing here establishes fabrication, concealment, explosives, motive or historical cause.

## Next records and preservation

[OPEN-TODOS.md](CITATIONS.md#nov04-310f3e31d3ab6357736c) carries the actual Boston cassette receipt/item/examination/retention chain, GTE/NYSP/FBI bridge/operator records, and the alleged Glick-associated recording and recipient/provider source chain. All five original tracks and eighteen full requirements remain open or partial. Acquired Keyton/usage/court components and the technical owners' completed tests remain recognized; no narrower completion criterion is adopted.

This is an additive public derivative of the native research report. Earlier source files, source conflicts, reports and public history remain unchanged. [CHECKLIST.md](CITATIONS.md#nov04-d9f399c872ce647b6151), [METHOD.md](CITATIONS.md#nov04-98f6534e515018636e96), [verification code/results](CITATIONS.md#nov04-1ed27f3115a6dbbec3c1) and the [actual preceding-text diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-b4ada6f83bd03ed22598) record the work and its limits. Raw scans, full extracts, cassette/audio, personnel/contact/signature information, masked identities, private case/correspondence and coordination records are excluded. No outreach, new request, fee, mailbox access, private push, merge, deletion or history rewrite was performed. Publication verifies disclosure of reviewed research, not substantive completion of the investigation.

# A public transcript reproduction supplies a recording item ID and conflicting accounts

October 31, 2026, America/New_York; captures extend into November 1 UTC. **TEL01-TEL04 / existing AUD016-A1, TC05-B105 and TC06-B110.** The ordinary public catalogue returned a 21-page reproduction matching the cited title. Its indexed/OCR representation supplies **item 1A-1A64148**, **SOURCE_ACCN 2496**, and Bates range **M-NKA-00012169–00012189**. These are more precise retrieval keys for the underlying media and records. The actual original audio, envelope, native transcript and receipt remain unacquired.

## Source form and actual reading

[Public record 18886478](https://www.scribd.com/document/18886478/T7-B12-Flight-93-Calls-Todd-Beamer-Fdr-9-15-01-FBI-302-Transcript-UAL-SAC-Nick-Leonard-Re-Jefferson-Beamer-Call-419) is a third-party mirror under a 9/11 Document Archive upload attribution. Its title is a catalogue pointer, not proof of official custodianship. Its actual pages display indexed fields, an OCR section, transcript text and privacy markings. We did not obtain an authenticated original FD340 facsimile, a master PDF or audio. No native PDF/audio hash is claimed.

Normal public search/page/zoom controls exposed the text without sign-in or export. One catalogue query and one matching-result open were used; the second planned query was unnecessary. After an initial four-page check, the reading scope was prospectively extended to the same complete 21-page representation to understand its nested records. All 21 reported Bates labels and the end-of-tape text were read/captured through the ordinary reader. Twelve selected viewport JPEGs across ten viewer pages support the important fields; these are not 21 complete facsimile images. Contact-pattern lines were omitted from the printed reading aid, and no incidental family details are adopted here.

The preserved browser-decoded UTF-8 source capture is **65,271 bytes**, SHA-256 **f97c92e565db27da6a7445a4e2114163ddb8320c5c4f52a73b4712ab43e0eeeb**. This fingerprints that capture, not an original PDF, recording or FBI master. Raw privacy-marked images, reader text and full speech remain outside this public payload. [Source/capture fingerprints](CITATIONS.md#nov04-a1a0fc746daae47a6653), [actual scope](CITATIONS.md#nov04-04c6481222fc1d4f9cd7), [page/Bates/paragraph and capture-line pins](CITATIONS.md#nov04-80c2cdd469a091c17af1). Use the reader's Jump to Page control for the viewer-page locators below; captured DOM line numbers are separate from archival pagination.

## 1. The evidence index is more specific than the earlier promise

Viewer page 1/Bates 12169 displays **FBIITEMNO 1A-1A64148**, **EVIDENCETYPE 1A**, **P_SEIZDATE 09/15/2001** and **P_DESCR “RECORDING OF CONVERSATION”**. The item repeats in later footers. This is displayed index/OCR content, not independent proof of a seizure or physical receipt on that date. Handwritten/garbled received-date text and masked agent fields were not accepted or reconstructed.

The previously read [official September 22 FD302, physical 47-48](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-01-of-08/at_download/file#page=47) describes a September 15 follow-up being taped, a synopsis copy in 1A, and a recording copy to be provided later. Preserve that future-copy wording beside the new index date. No original envelope/receipt or media hash currently proves whether these concern the same copy, what the date field means operationally, or when material entered FBI custody. Do not convert either source into a controlling receipt date.

The mirror header on viewer page 3/Bates 12171 reads **285A-NY-280350-NK**. The official September 22 form reads **265A-NY-280350-NK**. Both source readings remain. The mirror's indexed/OCR form makes the original facsimile and case/item/Bates crosswalk especially important. An OCR/index/transcription issue, a different record assignment or another explanation remains untested. This is a provenance/retrieval difference, not established historical misfiling or tampering. No redacted identities are filled from this or another release.

## 2. Two conversations and a letter require separate source roles

Viewer pages 4-6/Bates 12172-12174 begin with a United Airlines representative reading a letter. The opening uses transcript terminology; the cover being read calls its content a **“text recollection.”** The described three-page letter contains a cover, contact information and an account; the reader skips the contact page. A letter dated September 14 is being read, which does not independently date the recording of that reading. S-A-C is the representative's stated UAL designation; its expansion and any claimed FBI authority are not established here.

Viewer page 7/Bates 12175 then notes a pause, introduces another conversation, mentions a received summary and asks to record the conversation. The document's recording label cannot collapse these into an original in-flight recording, one continuous call, or an authenticated single cassette. A transcript of a later reading is not the original written enclosure. Original audio, typed transcript, letter/fax and source-version/copy records remain separate targets.

Viewer page 18/Bates 12186 contains a negative response to a question about a tape and an explanation that local recording would have required leaving the floor/going to an office. Preserve the scope: this is a reported local-recording account, not a demonstrated absence of every possible carrier, facility or FBI relay capture. It provides contrary context to an assumption that the original in-flight call must have been recorded and then withheld. The facility policy/configuration and surviving media are still unverified.

## 3. Preserve the numerical and wording differences

| Question | Letter-reading segment | Later dialogue / other source | What remains untested |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Front/back count | Viewer 5/Bates 12173: **27 front / 10 back** | Viewer 10/Bates 12178: **10 front / 27 back** | Original letter and audio/transcription, source of counts and actual manifest/locations. Both sums are 37 only as arithmetic of reported values. Neither value is selected. |
| Conversation length | No independently measured duration obtained | Viewer 14/Bates 12182: initial **about 40 minutes**, then a **10-minute** reference attributed to others, then **15 minutes** | These are separate statements, not an adopted 10–15 range or calibrated duration. Who supplied the later figure and what each measured remain unknown. |
| End/held-line descriptions | Viewer 6/Bates 12174 describes silence while connected, followed by an ending at **nine a.m.** | Viewer 19-20/Bates 12187-12188 describe **about 10 more minutes** holding and later radio news **about 10 minutes later**. The [earlier official operator account](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/29ee78c52ae09a2cadeb33b0856c654c51982a3c/investigation/broader-evidence/fbi-vault-operator-record-2026-10-31/README.md) records a **20-minute** silent-open phase and a separate **approximately 5-minute** later-contact estimate. | Event/leg boundaries, recording and clock alignment are missing. No arithmetic timeline or controlling duration is imposed. |
| Phrase attribution | Viewer 6/Bates 12174 leaves a phrase to an unspecified speaker | Viewer 15/Bates 12183 contains a question/answer attributing a later wording to Todd | Original audio, notes and synopsis versions are needed. Do not silently make different reported wording a single verbatim last statement. |

The later dialogue also reports a call time of **about 8:45 central standard time** at viewer 8/Bates 12176. The letter-reading has an 8:45 a.m. label. Neither establishes a calibrated timebase, EST/EDT conversion or a match to a particular native row. Retellings and billing fields cannot be joined by a desired time alone. [All retained values and discriminating tests](CITATIONS.md#nov04-3123b149fc093cd06105).

## 4. Later knowledge, service claims and contrary evidence

Viewer pages 13-14/Bates 12181-12182 distinguish prior service use/corporate-program information, facts learned later and an inference about passengers discussing action together. Those distinctions matter for independence: a later detail is not automatically something independently heard during the original call. The actual provider/account records, initial notes, synopsis and recording versions should test that chain.

Viewer 19/Bates 12187 reports information told to the speaker about handling **over 200 simultaneous calls** in ordinary winter-storm conditions, **360** that day, and a lock-up described as short of a total crash. This is an anecdotal/reported technical account. Over 200 is not a hard 200 maximum, and 360 is not an authenticated incident metric. Native fault/recovery/session/clock logs are needed before using the passage to explain any particular billed or held duration.

The dialogue's calmness discussion, expressed uncertainty, later-learning disclosures and local-recording explanation are contrary evidence to treating every difference as an admission of fakery or concealed reconstruction. The ending discusses prospective press contact and information sharing; it does not itself establish a false account, foreknowledge or attack coordination. Different recollections, source copying, transcription/OCR problems and other causes remain untested. No preferred cause is adopted.

## 5. Processing dates are not one historical event

Viewer 21/Bates 12189 displays **INPUTBATCH: 1st Batch of 1A1B1C's delivered in August**, **RPTTEXT01: 1**, **EDIT_DATE: 10/30/2003**, **CREATE_DATE: 08/19/2003**, and **SOURCE_ACCN: 2496**. The 1 belongs to RPTTEXT01; the field does not say delivery on August 1. August has no supplied year/day. The footer elsewhere displays May 10, 2004 11:57 a.m.. Preserve those processing/footer labels separately from P_SEIZDATE 09/15/2001, the September 14 letter date and September 22 interview/copy promise.

No label alone authenticates a delivery recipient/date, physical custody, a particular Commission request, an edit to the historical account, or the completeness of a production. The original schema, batch manifest and actual receipt/version records remain missing. The 21-page container and inclusive 12169–12189 Bates range agree arithmetically; the transcript header/final page separately display internal PAGE 1 / PAGE 19. Different pagination is not proof of missing pages, and matching totals do not authenticate content or audio.

## Next originals and the full work plan

Highest retrieval value: item **1A-1A64148**, **SOURCE_ACCN 2496**, the exact Bates range, original released FD340/transcript facsimiles, actual media/copy/receipt/disposition and the original letter/synopsis. Then compare recorded words and source versions with the conflicting counts, duration/phase descriptions and attribution. Native carrier/operator/recipient/clock/usage/exhibit joins and the GTE paper/Excel 97 diskette remain required. No native audio, original letter, master PDF, confirmed custody or independent clock/vendor test is claimed here.

[OPEN-TODOS.json](CITATIONS.md#nov04-9e5e7caba71280f37277) preserves all five tracks and original 18 requirements. FAA drafting/knowledge/corrected QFR/receipt/signed-action and separate recommendation-outcome originals remain open. Recorder/materials native-input and empirical tests remain with their technical owner. Steel preservation/transfer/native inventory/attribution and contractor-originals remain open. This source acquisition and reading advances TEL provenance and retrieval; it closes none of the original 18 criteria.

High confidence attaches to the displayed fields and the retained captures within their stated scope. Fidelity to original documents/media, complete custody and historical event truth remain unverified. The next originals could weaken or strengthen particular interpretations; source count, arithmetic or publication is not proof of conspiracy. Earlier contradictory observations, sources, reports, shared registers/workplan and public history remain unchanged.

[Reading/checklist](CITATIONS.md#nov04-66fe20eca7a70a0dc9f7), [verification limits](CITATIONS.md#nov04-d64aad0c94df9f5257fa) and [actual addition diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-0ec681425cd0f1ab384d) accompany the notes. Only appropriate original research, selected safe values and source links form this redundant public derivative. Raw privacy-marked source captures/full speech, incidental personal/contact/financial/access/signature data, private case material and credentials are excluded. No account/gate bypass, outreach, fees, new request/filing, mailbox access, experiment, source overwrite, deletion, private push or history rewrite occurred. Publication is established separately by the actual AveLyra account/commit/ref/all-file receipts; no independent audit acceptance is implied.

# Charlotte's SERO evidence inventory identifies original media, statements and copy routes

October 31, 2026, America/New_York; reading November 1 UTC. **TEL01–TEL04 / AUD016-A1, TC05-B105 and TC06-B110.** The Woodward original-note search did not recover his signed notes. It instead led to previously uninspected pages in an already held compilation: a September 13, 2001 Charlotte FBI communication describing a separate **AA11/Ong/SERO** evidence chain. It names original-format media, original statements, system/configuration backup and particular copy/transfer routes. The underlying media, statements, receipts and native logs remain unacquired; this is a more precise retrieval map, not an authenticated call reconstruction.

## Source and exact reading boundary

The [public PDF mirror](https://www.les-crises.fr/wp-content/uploads/2016/06/Team7-Box13-Flight-Call-Notes-and-302s.pdf#page=30) is a third-party reproduction of FBI records, acquired October 27 UTC: **2,997,662 bytes**, SHA-256 **e3cf374351cb753d1297433f86bb5807c56cb42a6dc69837a9df02dd0d54f4aa**. That hash identifies the preserved mirror body, not native agency custody or original media. All **eight EC pages**, physical **30–37**, a header page followed by printed internal pages **2–8**, footers **000000429–000000436**, were visually read at **140 dpi**. The other 65 compilation pages were not comprehensively read. Existing ordinary text served only as a locator; no new OCR, hidden/redacted-text recovery or fresh PDF download.

The initial plan mistakenly assumed the first five identified pages completed the communication. Page 34 visibly continued. Before further image reading, an existing-index boundary check located its end and the scope was prospectively extended to eight images. The initial plan, five-page qualification and later extension remain preserved locally. No unread page is claimed read, and completing this EC is not a complete-production or native-authentication finding. [Source/image fingerprints](CITATIONS.md#nov04-052f27c065872a7b0eef), [page/item pins](CITATIONS.md#nov04-634fada0afa011e2381d), [actual scope](CITATIONS.md#nov04-e35e8fb8f37c78050e6e).

## Precise case and holder references

**Physical 30/header page** prints **09/13/2001**, From **Charlotte / Raleigh Resident Agency**, case **265D-NY-280350-CE (Pending)**. It references **265D-NY-280350 Serial 187**, **DL267**, **CE66** and **CE233**. Serial 187 is a referenced lead/record, not proved to be this EC's own serial. Pending is a historical label, not present case status. The administrative paragraph says Charlotte retained the original evidence it obtained. It addresses the Director's Office/SIOC and several field offices; address-list inclusion is not demonstrated receipt. P01.

## Original media and statements, with their labels intact

The inventory on **physical 30–32/header and printed 2–3** identifies:

- **Item 1(A):** an original CD-ROM recording of the Ong call in **WAV** format. **Item 1(B):** an original CD-ROM recording in both a **Rockwell Industries format** and **WAV Format**. These are the inventory's labels, not inspected files, known codecs or two independently captured calls. Both sit under a holding labelled **1B**; item 1(A) is not itself a classification of 1A evidence. P02.
- **Item 3(A–H):** eight statement entries with handwritten, typed and otherwise unspecified forms. The list expressly distinguishes an **unsigned** typed version and a **signed** typed version for one named participant, and original/typed versions for other participants. Eight entries do not mean eight independent witnesses or eight separate corroborations. No actual statement or signature was inspected. P03.
- **Item 4:** one original **4 mm DDS 90 tape** described as snapshots of the automated call distribution system **as of 0130EST, 9/13/01**, containing **ACD Operation System** and **ACD configuration settings**. Preserve the September 13 date and EST label. This is not demonstrated September 11 effective configuration, calibrated time, call audio, a complete event archive or a validated forensic system image. P04.

These are separate media, statement and configuration targets. A digital carrier/storage description does not authenticate the contents, and the word original in an inventory does not prove a bit-level native acquisition or full custody chain.

## Copies, interview notes, activity reports and future dispatch

**Physical 32–34/internal 3–5** lists copies of interview/transcription reports as enclosures. It also says original FD-302s and specified **1A** materials would be sent separately **at a future date**: work-copy CD-ROMs, original FBI interview notes and **AA Activity Reports for Minter and Sadler** described as showing exact call times. The word exact is a source characterization, not verified calibration. Original operator statements, FBI interview notes, report copies, work-copy audio and original-format media remain distinct. A promise of future dispatch is not actual dispatch, delivery, receipt or present custody. P05.

**Physical 36/internal 7, first paragraph** reports copies of documentation passing through AA's Dallas Security Headquarters to the FBI Dallas Division, while original versions and a system copy on hard media were placed with Charlotte. This gives additional reported copy-holder routes. It does not establish identical bytes, an intact receipt trail, delivery of the promised 1A package or a complete forensic image. P06.

The last paragraph on **physical 37/internal 8** says the particular Serial-187 Cary/Charlotte leads should be considered covered. That is not a global FBI closure, completed recipient delivery, complete records search, destruction authorization or proof every laboratory/technical question was answered. P07.

## Time sources, recording limit and routing uncertainty

**Physical 34/internal 5** explicitly says a witness's remembered call time differed from AA telephone-system records and that times in the EC were estimated using the records custodian's knowledge rather than the interviewed witnesses. The same page uses **approximately 8:20 A.M.** earlier, then **precisely 8:20 A.M., EST** for recording start and **approximately 8:44 A.M., EST** for call end. Preserve the source's changing precision, without choosing a controlling timestamp, converting EST/EDT or aligning it to a desired event. P08.

**Physical 32, enclosure 10** describes a four-minute transcription of an approximate twenty-five (25) call; that particular phrase omits an explicit duration unit. **Physical 35/internal 6** explicitly describes a **25-minute** call. Both readings remain; no unit is silently inserted into the earlier line and no exact duration is calculated from approximate clock claims. P09.

The EC attributes the **four-minute recording stop** to a pre-programmed default timer, while describing a longer call. **Physical 36** reports a negative check for an additional automatic quality-control recording and describes the same four-minute limit for a separate unrelated call. These are reported technical/search accounts from one communication, not independent configuration tests. The original audio, native settings, audit/change history, extraction method and alternate-recording search logs were not inspected. No deliberate truncation or complete absence of other recordings is established. P10.

**Physical 35–36/internal 6–7** records a manager's initial assumption about a direct shortcut route, a reported AT&T Digital no-record response for such a call, and proposed routing through the general reservations number using either a cellular or flight phone. Preserve all accounts. This is a provider response relayed through another person; the actual carrier query, scope, raw result and call-leg join are missing. It does not establish that no call occurred, select cellular versus onboard service, or transfer SERO/Rockwell receiving-system semantics to GTE ACIS or another flight. The exact shortcut/destination numbers are not reproduced here. P11.

## Next records and evidentiary strength

The discriminating originals are: the two described media items and their format/export histories; the DDS backup and associated extraction/version/change records; individual original statements versus FBI interview notes; the two operators' activity reports and native time definitions; the promised 1A-package dispatch/receipt; Charlotte/Dallas/SIOC copy manifests; and the actual AT&T route-query and alternate-recording search records. Reproduced inventory, narrative and transmittal passages from this single chain are not independent corroboration of the call or its timing.

The [Woodward signed-note target](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/e357f0778da4001ee9ea41d56ce77bae974e862f/investigation/broader-evidence/telephone-signed-notes-continuations-2026-10-31/README.md), the separate Jefferson Post-it notes and original letter/media, and all native caller/operator/recipient/clock/exhibit joins remain open. The EC's unrelated customer/reservation allegations and personal information are excluded from this public review; no ethnicity-based or identity inference is adopted. None of these records establishes fabrication, concealment, foreknowledge, coordination or attack participation. The listed originals could support, qualify or contradict the reports.

All original five tracks and 18 requirements remain open or partial. New progress is source-level identification of native media/configuration, note, copy-holder and timing-method targets; source counts, hashes and publication are not historical proof or completion. [Comparison](CITATIONS.md#nov04-b16138300da50ae3533f), [TODOs](CITATIONS.md#nov04-ac8942dbac56f005b1d6), [checklist](CITATIONS.md#nov04-5940743f0ee54f76080c), [verification limits](CITATIONS.md#nov04-f988244debc23548d4d8) and [addition diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-de1bafd95cee2a37d3cb) accompany the safe redundant notes. Raw marked PDFs/images/text, signatures, personal/contact/financial data, private case/correspondence/strategy and credentials are excluded. No outreach, fees, new request/filing, experiment, mailbox, deletion, overwrite, private push or history rewrite.

# An FBI interview continuation identifies signed and dated call notes

October 31, 2026, America/New_York; reading November 1 UTC. **TEL01–TEL04 / AUD016-A1, TC05-B105 and TC06-B110.** Three previously unread continuation-page images in the held official FBI releases were inspected. One supplies a specific original-record target: notes Woodward reportedly made during the Sweeney conversation, signed and dated, and gave to the interviewing agent. The notes themselves, their item number and present retention remain unverified.

## The handoff statement is a record lead, not the recovered notes

[Part02 physical page 25](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-02-of-08/at_download/file#page=25), internal page **2**, dated **09/11/01**, names **Michael Woodward** and displays case **265A-NY-280350-302**. Its third body paragraph says he made notes while speaking with Sweeney, signed and dated them, and gave them to the interviewing agent. The footer shows **REQ. #35-13** and **000000391**. This is an interview report's handoff statement, not an attached original note, inspected signature, intake receipt or proof of present FBI custody. P01.

[Part02 physical page 57](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-02-of-08/at_download/file#page=57) reproduces the selected same body claims, named witness, printed date, case and internal-page label, with footer **000000586** and the same request tag. Preserve both footer labels and separate page-image hashes. They are two reproduced versions of the account, not two independent witnesses, two handoffs or proof of two note sets. No whole-document/byte equivalence or cross-redaction reconstruction was performed. P02.

The next discriminator is the actual signed/dated note set and its attachment, intake, copying, transfer and retention records. A declared handoff is more specific than a generic reference to missing telephone evidence, but it does not authenticate the original notes' content or establish whether they agree with the interview summary. The original notes could corroborate, qualify or conflict with the later account. No preferred result is assumed.

## Keep the caller, artifact and service distinctions

The preceding paragraph on the same two pages reports Sweeney describing **Ong** speaking to someone on an air phone. That is a reported observation concerning a different caller. It does not itself identify the device or service Sweeney used, classify every AA11 call, validate a carrier row, or supply an authenticated recording. Do not transfer a device description from one caller to another. P03.

These Woodward/Sweeney notes are a separate record target from Jefferson's brief Post-it notes described in the [newly inspected May 11, 2004 memorandum](https://www.scribd.com/document/18886438/T7-B12-Flight-93-Calls-Todd-Beamer-Fdr-5-11-04-MFR-Lisa-Jefferson-416). The [preceding review](https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/b6923d4d9c874ab71fe88112f401d5279caaeb95/investigation/broader-evidence/telephone-operator-notes-mfr-2026-10-31/README.md) retains that reported FBI holding, its limited-value qualification and missing custody join. Neither note set is the typed synopsis, September 14 letter, carrier spreadsheet, recorded follow-up conversation or original in-flight audio. A specific original-note crosswalk is needed; a common agency or similar label is insufficient. P04.

## The Jefferson continuation preserves the relay and timing limits

[Part01 physical page 21](https://vault.fbi.gov/9-11%20Commission%20Report/9-11-interviews-2001-09-sep-01-of-08/at_download/file#page=21), internal page **2**, names **Lisa Jefferson**, prints **09/11/2001**, and displays case **265D-NY-280350**. Its footer shows **REQ. #35-13** and **000000128**. The first paragraph records a last exchange at **9:00 a.m. Central time**, then another **twenty minutes** of silent/open connection before disconnection. It also reports tracking equipment indicating service through a Pittsburgh transceiver. The transceiver claim is not independently checked aircraft position or a calibrated event clock. P05.

The second paragraph describes a separate Verizon relay call **approximately five minutes later**, estimated at **9:30 a.m. Central time**, continuing until **approximately 9:43 a.m.** It expressly says Jefferson could not hear the other passenger directly and received information second hand through the Verizon employee. Preserve this scope beside the later MFR's tentative account about another operator possibly listening to that other call. Different participants and legs have not been joined, and no contradiction or reconciliation is imposed. P06.

The **15-minute** silent/open estimate in the May 2004 MFR, the earlier **20-minute** account and the follow-up reproduction's **about 10 more minutes** remain separate source readings. These are not synchronized or summed into a timeline here. The newly read copy is not a new independent witness or a new measurement of an already documented estimate. The printed **265D** case is retained for this form; it is not silently substituted for the **265A** label on the different Woodward form or the later follow-up record. P07.

## Actual scope and remaining work

The three newly viewed images are **part01 physical 21** and **part02 physical 25/57**, rendered at **140 dpi** from the two already held official-hosted PDFs. No new download, OCR, whole-release rereading, hidden/redacted text recovery or detail crop was performed. Context first pages **20/24/56** were previously read; they are not counted again. The continuation headers provide printed internal-page, name/date and case context; adjacency alone is not used to authenticate a complete record or production. No native carrier, note, media, manifest or office log was acquired. [Source fingerprints](CITATIONS.md#nov04-1446b3eb0063141f3886), [pins](CITATIONS.md#nov04-17f51b7b2582f537c84d), [scope](CITATIONS.md#nov04-5436b0492cc9fca400d7).

High confidence attaches to the selected visible fields and handoff paragraph in these preserved copies. The reported historical handoff, exact note identity, completeness and present custody have not been independently authenticated. Reproductions, interview summaries and later recollections are not independent corroboration. No missing-record, false-call, concealment, motive, coordination or attack-participation conclusion follows from the note reference, clock estimates or different footer labels.

All original five tracks and 18 requirements remain open or partial. [TODOs](CITATIONS.md#nov04-68f62563f4751903d67c) retain the individual note sets, originals/receipts, complete production, letter/media and carrier/operator/clock/exhibit joins. [Comparison](CITATIONS.md#nov04-46663da5d242cee9158e), [checklist](CITATIONS.md#nov04-24a1400e2fd8192475c9), [verification limits](CITATIONS.md#nov04-2fc719676e37e34c4269) and [additive diff](CITATIONS.md#nov04-f36f6f0a753aac348a1f) preserve this supplement without altering prior evidence or public history. Only selected appropriate original research and source links are published; raw PDFs/images/extractions, personal/contact/financial/signature information, private correspondence/case material, litigation strategy and credentials remain excluded.
